Brand B: 20,000 current email subscribers. And they gain 250 new subscribers a month..

# GAMMA

Email Marketing Foundations

# **Email Marketing Foundations**

# **Profitable Ecommerce Funnel**

# **The State of The Market**

A lot has changed in the ecom world in the past 5 years…  
People have lots of money from stimmy checks → ecom bull market → more ecom brands come to market → consumer has less cash due to economy → ecom becomes more difficult

# **The Modern Customer Has Changed**

* The customer has unlimited options (thanks Shopify, they see 70+ ecom ads per day)  
* The customer LIKES to shop around… it's fun to see all these new brands \+ doom scrolling  
* The customer is less loyal than ever…

# **The Core Ecommerce Problems**

#### **Rising Cost Per Acquisition**

Increased ad demand resulting in higher CPMs \+ more competition making it difficult to acquire customers

#### **Decreased Customer Retention**

Customers have so many options to choose from they are quick to jump from your brand to the next

#### **Profitability**

Increased Cost Per Acquisition x Lower LTV x Tariffs \= Lower Profitability

At the end of the day… it all comes down to profitability.  
We need to build systems that lower our CAC and increase our LTV if we want to survive in this market at a profit.  
So… how do we get people to convert for cheap and stay for cheap?  
You guessed it… email\!

# **Funnel People Into The Cheaper Platforms**

We can't just run ads and hope people buy…  
That's too expensive. People don't just buy on your website the first time they visit us.  
Instead, we need to capture their information when they come to the site and then send them emails which are cheap as dirt to get them to convert.  
**Unprofitable Acquisition:** Pay for a person to see your ad 10 times before they convert.  
**Profitable Acquisition:** Pay for someone to see your ad once, click once, capture their email, and send them 10 emails for pennies to get then to convert.

# **Email / SMS Is The Key**

* **Highest ROI Channel** – Email averages **$36+ return for every $1 spent**, far outpacing ads. Once you own the list, sending costs almost nothing.  
* **You Own the Audience** – No algorithm or rising ad costs can take your list away. Every subscriber is a direct line to revenue.  
* **Compounds Over Time** – Every new email collected grows your reach permanently, creating a snowball effect for future sales.  
* **Drives Repeat Purchases** – Most profit in ecom comes from returning customers; email lets you bring them back without paying for ads again.  
* **End-to-End Control** – From first visit to post-purchase upsell, email lets you control the customer journey and maximize lifetime value.

# **3.5 Pillars Of Email Marketing**

# **All That Matters In Email Marketing**

# **Pillar \#1: List Growth**

Who's likely to make more money this year?

**Brand A**

10,000 current email subscribers

Gains 2,000 new subscribers/mo

**Brand B**

20,000 current email subscribers

Gains 250 new subscribers/mo

New subscribers coming in are one of the most important metrics of email marketing.  
Why?

* **Subscribers Naturally Churn Over Time**  
* Over time, people will naturally lose interest to your brand and your emails.  
* You always need to be replenishing your active subscribers at a higher rate than you're losing them.  
* **New Subscribers Are Much Warmer**  
* They just opted into your list from an ad, they're ready to buy.  
* Honeymoon phase \+ fresh interest

# **Pillar \#2: Email Flows**

Flows are the automated emails that get sent to a customer after they complete a specified action.  
Things like joining the email list, abandoning an item they added to cart, or making a purchase.  
Flows allow us to automate the customer journey and send high converting reminders, cross sells, and relevant information to the customer.  
They should make up around 50% of your total email revenue with the other 50% coming from campaigns.

* Automated revenue ✅  
* Take pressure off weekly campaigns ✅  
* Convert high intent traffic ✅  
* Recover lost sales (abandonment flows) ✅  
* Personalize customer experience ✅

# **Pillar \#3: Email Campaigns**

Campaigns are the one-time emails you send to your list at a specific date and time.  
It's the easiest way in ecommerce to get consistent, cheap traffic from customers who already know, like, and trust you.  
We need a consistent cadence of 2-4 email campaigns per week to establish a relationship with the consumer where they get used to seeing you in the inbox and in the habit of opening your emails.  
If you're sending good content that people enjoy such as tips, education, etc then people will look forward to your emails.

# **Pillar \#3.5: Deliverability**

Deliverability essentially refers to your domain and IP's sending "credit score".  
If you have a good credit score you'll land in the **primary tab**.  
If you have a bad credit score you'll land in **spam**.  
If you're somewhere in the middle you'll land in the **promotions tab**.  
The goal is to, of course, land in the primary tab.  
Why only a 3.5 pillar? Because it's easy\!  
As long as you only send to engaged profiles and send good content, you'll be fine with landing in the primary tab.

# **Revenue Attribution Metrics**

| Metric | Target |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Email % of Total Store Revenue** | 40**%** (Goal)30–50% is healthy\>55% \= time to scale acquisition |
| **Campaigns vs Flows** | **Campaigns:** 40–60% of email revenue**Flows:** 40–60% of email revenue If you're too reliant on one, you're leaving easy revenue on the table from the other. |

| **Spam Complaint Rates** | \<0.01% |
| **Pop-Up Form Submission Rate** | 6-12% |

# **Email Marketing Glossary & Key Terms**

# **Glossary And Key Terms**

### 💰 **Revenue Attribution & Metrics**

* **Email Revenue Attribution** – The percentage of your total store revenue that comes from email. Target is \~40%.  
* **Campaign Revenue** – Revenue generated from manual one-off emails.  
* **Flow Revenue** – Revenue from automated emails triggered by specific user behavior (abandoned cart, welcome, etc).  
* **LTV (Lifetime Value)** – The total revenue a customer will generate during their relationship with your brand.  
* **AOV (Average Order Value)** – Average spend per order. Often increased with upsells in flows or bundles in campaigns.  
* **ROI (Return on Investment)** – Email typically delivers \~40x ROI when done right.

### 📬 **Email Types**

* **Campaign** – A one-time broadcast email sent to a list or segment (e.g. promo, product launch, newsletter).  
* **Flow (Automation)** – A series of emails triggered by a customer action (e.g. checkout abandonment).  
* **Transactional Email** – Order confirmation, shipping notification, etc. Often not considered "marketing" but can be optimized.  
* **Text-Based Email** – Email with little to no images or design. Often converts better and improves deliverability.

### 🔄 **Core Automated Flows (Klaviyo-Specific but universal)**

* **Welcome Flow** – Triggered when someone joins your list. Purpose: Convert and onboard.  
* **Site Abandonment Flow** – Triggered when someone visits but doesn't click on products.  
* **Browse Abandonment Flow** – Triggered when a product is viewed but not added to cart.  
* **Cart Abandonment Flow** – Triggered when someone adds to cart but doesn’t check out.  
* **Checkout Abandonment Flow** – Triggered when checkout is started but not completed.  
* **Post-Purchase Flow** – Triggered after purchase. Builds loyalty, reduces remorse, and upsells.  
* **Winback Flow** – Triggered when a customer hasn’t purchased in a while. Goal: Re-engage and convert again.  
* **Sunset Flow** – Triggered when a contact is no longer engaging. Removes or suppresses inactive users.

### 🎯 **Segmentation & Targeting**

* **Segment** – A dynamic group of contacts who meet certain criteria (e.g. opened an email in the last 30 days).  
* **List** – A static group of contacts, typically from a specific source (e.g. quiz opt-in, lead gen).  
* **Engaged Segment** – A group of subscribers who have opened or clicked emails recently (14/30/60/90 day).  
* **Exclusion Segment** – A segment you exclude from campaigns (e.g. unengaged, buyers, VIPs).  
* **Tag** – Metadata attached to subscribers (e.g. gender, quiz results, etc.)  
* **Dynamic Segment** – Automatically updates as people meet criteria.  
* **Static Segment** – Manually added users, does not update automatically.  
* **VIP Segment** – High spenders, repeat buyers, or top 1% customers.  
* **Non-Buyers** – Subscribers who have never purchased.  
* **Multi-Buyers** – Customers who’ve purchased 2+ times.  
* **Segment Conditions** – Rules that define who enters a segment.  
* **Personalization Tokens** – Dynamic fields like {{ first\_name }} that personalize the email.

### 📈 **Performance Metrics**

* **Open Rate** – % of people who opened your email. Target: 45%+ for engaged segments.  
* **Click Rate (CTR)** – % of total recipients who clicked. Target: 2–4%+.  
* **Click-to-Open Rate (CTOR)** – % of openers who clicked. Measures email effectiveness.  
* **Conversion Rate** – % of recipients who completed a purchase.  
* **Revenue per Recipient (RPR)** – Revenue divided by total recipients.  
* **EPC (Earnings Per Click)** – Revenue earned per click.  
* **Unsubscribe Rate** – % of people who unsubscribed. Target: \<0.2%.  
* **Bounce Rate** – % of emails not delivered. Target: \<2%.  
* **Spam Complaint Rate** – % of users who mark your email as spam. Target: \<0.1%.  
* **Deliverability** – Likelihood that your emails land in the inbox (vs. spam or promotions tab).

### 🧼 **List Health & Deliverability**

* **List Cleaning** – Removing cold subscribers who haven’t engaged in 60–90+ days.  
* **Suppression List** – List of contacts who should never be emailed again.  
* **Warmup (or Rewarm)** – Process of gradually rebuilding sender reputation when deliverability is low.  
* **Spam Trap** – Fake emails used by inbox providers to catch spammers.  
* **Smart Sending** – Klaviyo feature that skips sending to people recently emailed. **Turn off for flows**\!  
* **Domain Reputation** – Score based on how inbox providers trust your email-sending behavior.  
* **Hard Bounce** – Email cannot be delivered permanently (invalid address).  
* **Soft Bounce** – Temporary failure (inbox full, server issue).  
* **Sender Reputation** – Inbox providers’ trust score for your domain.  
* **DKIM / SPF / DMARC** – Email authentication protocols.  
* **Re-engagement Campaign** – Emails trying to win back cold subscribers.  
* **Inbox Placement** – Where the email lands: inbox, promotions, spam.

### 🛠 **Platform & Design Terms**

* **Klaviyo** – The most used eCommerce email platform, especially for Shopify.  
* **ESP (Email Service Provider)** – Software used to send marketing emails (e.g., Klaviyo, Mailchimp).  
* **Subject Line** – Main headline in the inbox.  
* **Preview Text** – Secondary text that appears next to subject line in inbox.  
* **Call-to-Action (CTA)** – The link or button that encourages the reader to take action.  
* **A/B Testing** – Testing two versions of an email (subject, design, copy, etc) to see which performs better.  
* **Dynamic Blocks** – Personalized content shown based on data (e.g., gender, location, product viewed).  
* **Conditional Split** – A rule in a flow that changes the email path depending on subscriber behavior or attributes.  
* **Alt Text** – Descriptive text shown when an image fails to load.  
* **Responsive Design** – Optimized for both desktop and mobile.  
* **Mobile-First Layout** – Designed to perform best on mobile devices.  
* **Email Width** – Standard width is 600–700px to ensure compatibility.  
* **Modular Design** – Repeatable blocks you can mix and match.

### 🧠 **Copywriting & Content**

* **Direct Response Copy** – Copy meant to drive immediate action.  
* **Long-Form Email** – Detailed storytelling email (usually \>250 words).  
* **Skimmable Copy** – Designed for fast, mobile-first reading.  
* **Bridge Section** – Visual/graphic transition that keeps readers scrolling.  
* **Bullet Block** – List of short benefits or features.  
* **Hero Section** – Top of email, usually contains headline \+ main CTA.  
* **Microcopy** – Tiny bits of text like CTA button copy or disclaimers.  
* **P.S. Section** – Postscript, often used for urgency, testimonials, or CTAs.

### 🧪 **Testing & Optimization**

* **Multivariate Testing** – Testing multiple variables (e.g. subject \+ design).  
* **Heatmap** – Shows where users click or scroll in your emails.  
* **Clickmap** – Visual tool to track which links/buttons get the most clicks.  
* **Time Delay** – Wait time between automated flow emails.

### 📊 **Analytics & Attribution**

* **UTM Parameters** – URL tags for tracking in Google Analytics.  
* **Klaviyo Revenue Attribution** – Revenue tracked back to an email send.  
* **Last Click Attribution** – Revenue attributed to last click before purchase.  
* **Open Attribution** – Assigns value to emails that were opened before purchase.  
* **Custom Metrics** – Track specific actions (e.g., quiz completions).  
* **Predictive Analytics** – Klaviyo feature for estimating next order date, churn risk, etc.

### 🔐 **Compliance & Consent**

* **Opt-In** – When a user gives permission to receive emails.  
* **Double Opt-In** – User confirms their subscription via a second email.  
* **GDPR** – EU law regulating data and email marketing consent.  
* **CAN-SPAM** – U.S. law requiring unsubscribe links and contact info.  
* **CASL** – Canada’s anti-spam law.  
* **Unsubscribe Link** – Required link to opt out of future emails.  
* **Preference Center** – Page where users can choose what types of emails they want to receive.

### 💡 **Strategy & Frameworks**

* **Value Stacking** – Showing multiple benefits to increase perceived product value.  
* **Offer Ladder** – Moving from low-friction opt-in to higher ticket offers.  
* **Customer Journey** – Mapping email content to each stage (awareness, consideration, purchase, retention).  
* **Trust Threshold** – The moment a customer finally trusts you enough to purchase.  
* **Margin Preservation** – Making sales *without* using discounts (via value-based or content-driven emails).  
* **List Monetization** – Strategies to convert subscribers into customers over time.  
* **The 80/20 Rule** – Focus on the flows or campaigns that drive the majority of revenue.  
* **Content Buckets** – Themes you can rotate weekly (e.g. education, social proof, product drop, lifestyle).  
* **Email Calendar** – A scheduled map of campaigns by week/month.

Email Marketing Foundations

# GAMMA LIST

List Growth

# **List Growth**

# **The Types Of List Growth**

# **The 4 Methods of List Growth**

### **Pop-Up Forms**

Will be your \#1 driver of list-growth and where we will be spending a lot of time.

### **Post-Purchase Opt Ins**

This is a simple checkbox at checkout which will sign up someone's email for receiving marketing.

### **Sign-Up Pages**

Sign up pages are full pages that you can link on your site and on socials to get email sign-ups.

### **Sign-Up Embeds**

Sign up embeds are email address sign ups build inside other pages (generally your footer or your homepage).

# **Post-Purchase Opt-Ins**

This is a simple checkbox at checkout which will sign up someone's email for receiving marketing.  
SUPER low hanging fruit that takes 5 minutes to set up on Shopify.  
We want it to be auto-checked so someone will be added to the list.  
This allows you to always grab info and market to the people who already know, like and trust you.

* From your Shopify admin, go to **Settings** \> [**​Checkout​**](https://admin.shopify.com/settings/checkout).  
* To add a sign-up checkbox to your checkout, in the **Marketing options** section, check **Email**.  
* Check **Preselected** so that the email marketing sign-up check box is preselected at the checkout by default for customers without an account or customers who are on your email subscription list. The email marketing sign-up check box isn't preselected for customers who have opted out of email marketing or who aren't on your email subscription list.  
* Click **Save**.

# **Sign-Up Pages**

Sign up pages are full pages that you can link on your site and on socials.  
They are always great to have in your website footer and in social media bios.  
Tip: Remove as much friction as possible\!

* From your Shopify admin, go to **Online Store** \> [**​Themes​**](https://admin.shopify.com/themes).  
* Find the theme that you want to edit, and then click **Customize**.  
* Click **Add section**.  
* Click **Newsletter**, then click **Add**.  
* Enter a heading for your newsletter signup. For example, "Subscribe to our newsletter".  
* In the subheading field, enter a short description of what your customer will receive by subscribing.  
* Click **Save**.

# **General Sign Up Embeds**

Sign up embeds are email address sign ups build inside other pages (generally your footer or your homepage).  
These are easy ways to get quick signups and grow you list passively.

* From your Shopify admin, go to **Online Store** \> [**​Themes​**](https://admin.shopify.com/themes).  
* Find the theme that you want to edit, and then click **Customize**.  
* Click **Add section**.  
* Click **Newsletter**, then click **Add**.  
* Enter a heading for your newsletter signup. For example, "Subscribe to our newsletter".  
* In the subheading field, enter a short description of what your customer will receive by subscribing.  
* Click **Save**.

# **The Importance Of Pop-Up Forms**

# **With No / Low Converting Pop-Up**

People just aren't ready to buy when they first visit your site.  
New visitor conversion rates are sub 2%.  
So… you're left with only a few bucks from the people who ARE ready to buy right then and there.  
The others will just **leave and likely never come back.**

# **With A High Converting Pop-Up**

A high converting pop-up will allow us to capture the folks we paid so much cash to acquire.  
Rather than asking to get married right off the bat (making a purchase), we're starting with a quick date (getting their email).  
Then, educate them through email with and **convert them after we have their trust and their objections are addressed.**

# **Real Life Example**

This is a real client example after increasing their **pop-up conversion rate from 2.5% to 8.75%**.  
Keep in mind, we didn't touch any of their emails.  
As a result, their monthly Welcome Email Flow revenue went from **$7,000 —\> $25,000/mo**  
A direct 3.5x pop-up conversion rate to a 3.5x increase in monthly welcome flow revenue.  
Over 12 months this will equate to **31,500 subscribers** (more people on your list to continually sell to) compared to 9,000.  
And **$300,000 of revenue** compared to $84,000.  
This doesn't take into account the new subscribers who will buy multiple times throughout the year.

|  | Before (3 Month Averages) | After |
| :---- | :---: | :---: |
| Average Order Value | \~ $93 | \~ $93 |
| Monthly Site Visitors | \~ 30,000 | \~ 30,000 |
| Form Conversion Rate | **2.5%** | **8.75%** |
| New Email Subscribers | **750** | **2,625** |
| Welcome Flow Revenue | **$7,000** | **$25,000** |

# **Set and Forget \= Burning Cash**

No matter your form, it can be improved.  
It's only through testing, iterating, improving, etc that you reach 10%+ opt in rates.  
Even your 10%+ forms should continue to be tested to try to reach 20%+.  
In reality, it will take many new tests to really get one that sticks.

# **How To Create An Effective Pop-Up Form**

# **Steps to Creating A Pop-Up Form**

### **Establish Offer**

Choose what incentive you will be giving recipients.

### **Choose What Form Type You Want**

Use one of the 5 form types to pick a starting point.

### **Create Your Form in Klaviyo / Alia**

Use my tutorial guides to create your form in your desired platform.

### **Run Your Form Through My Checklist**

Use my checklist to be sure there are no stones unturned and your form is fully optimized.

### **Set Up A/B Tests**

Set up your variations, monitor results, and iterate upon your learnings.

# **1\. Establish Your Offer**

Your offer is your most important aspect of your pop-up form.  
Try different offers based off your margins and opt-in rates.  
A great test to ask yourself… **if I was given this offer would I exchange my email for it?**  
Make sure you test these different styles:

* **Main Options**  
* % OFF (choose if your AOV is below $100)  
* $ OFF (choose if your AOV above $100)  
* Free Shipping  
* Free X Gift w/ Order  
* BONUS: Use a "Mystery Discount" in your sign-up and give them one of the above options  
* **Other Options**  
* Giveaway Entry  
* Free Guide  
* Early Access

# **2\. Choose What Type Of Form You Want**

Different forms will have different effectiveness based on your audience.  
It's important to give all of these forms a shot to see what resonates the best.  
Start with one and test from there.  
Your best bet to start is going to be "The Quiz" or "The Micro Commit"

# **3\. Create Your Form in Klaviyo / Alia**

[**​Klaviyo​**](https://www.klaviyo.com/partners/signup?utm_medium=partner&utm_source=001Nu0000022YR4IAM)Tutorials will be provided in a future module.  
Klaviyo and ​Alia​ different platforms for your pop-ups and the only ones I'd recommend.  
Alia is the superior option and will perform much better, but it's an extra cost (the ROI is worth it every time).

* **Say Max sent you when you book a call and you'll get a gift ;)**  
* [**​https://www.alialearn.com/​**](https://www.alialearn.com/)

# **4\. Successful Pop-Up Checklist**

* Only asks for one input per step (ask for email then sms in separate steps)  
* Time delay is 4-12 seconds  
* Form loads instantly after delay with no lag  
* Form covers at least 75% of screen  
* Under 10 words of copy  
* Offer is the largest aspect of the form  
* OPTIONAL: There is no "x" button, rather a "Close Form" button underneath the submit button

# **Top 7 Best A/B Tests To Run On Your Forms**

### **Offer**

Highest lever you can test. Try the different offers outlined above [**​h​**](https://gamma.app/docs/bvf3tdaoibnpn2d#card-dmyr7ojqfk6rxgh)and even try doing a "Mystery Offer"

### **Form Type**

Another large lever. Try the different form types ​above.​ You can even test out using a pop-up vs full page vs flyout inside of Klaviyo.

### **Close Form Functionality**

Try using a close form button vs an x in the corner of your form. You can even test the placement of your close buttons or x.

### **Time Delay**

Use different time delays to see what maximizes TOTAL submits, not conversion rate. Try 4 second vs 12 second to start then start closing the gap.

### **Photo Content**

Use different photos using different content. Some photos may appeal more than others. You should even test the placement of photos either next to the form or as a backdrop to the form.

### **Copy**

Test out the type of copy you use. Try longer forms vs shorter forms with copy varying from "Unlock my offer" to "You've got xyz".

### **Colors**

Not a massive lever, but can make a difference. Typically the less colors the better.

# **Great Examples**

Here's a swipe file of [**​29 solid pop-up form examples.​**](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1yxVQ-rSIyDgbWgqmlmGs4_VlmD_acE0k?usp=sharing)

Transcrição do Vídeo :   
\# File-Welcome Flow

Alrighty, debatably the most important flow. We have the welcome flow. Let's get into it.

So, the cost per acquisition problem. Just want to talk a little bit about the pop-up and the welcome flow.

The CAC problem. You cannot lower ad costs. It's impossible. Zuck has us in a chokehold and he can make you pay whatever he wants for you to make a purchase.

But what you can do is you can make every click that you get off those ads worth more.

With an improper system in place, you're not going to get as much off of your click.

With a dialed pop-up form, you're able to turn your paid traffic into owned traffic.

You're able to get their email to send them messages.

And a powerful welcome flow, which is going to trigger after somebody opts in, is going to turn those curiosity people into conversions.

It is the cheapest and highest leverage way to stretch your ad dollars.

That is with your pop-up form and then your welcome flow.

A lot of brands, it's more like 10, 15, 5\.

But you pay $50, at least get the email, build trust, and then convert them now or later without having to pay again on ads.

That is how you beat rising CAC.

Get them to click once on an ad, get their email address, and send them emails for cheap.

What happens if you don't nail this?

Somebody goes to your website.

You miss the trust window where somebody, right when they see you, you miss this.

You get subscribers that abandon, never come back, and future purchases lost forever.

So, before versus after, funnel fixer, without pop-up and welcome flow, $50 in ad spend.

You get clicks.

People bounce off your website.

You don't get any email captured, lost money.

With pop-up form and welcome flow, you get clicks, and at least you get the email captured.

You get to send those welcome emails that educate people and have urgency and direct response.

You get a sale today, or you prime people for sales later on, and then you get increased LTV off of your ad clickers.

So, instead of just like LTV for customers, LTV on-site visitors.

Same ad spend, but completely different outcome just by optimizing your pop-up and your welcome flow.

So, the welcome flow.

Non-negotiables.

This is what we want.

Every welcome flow is going to be completely different.

I don't want to just give you this set and play template because a brand that's selling $10,000 saunas is going to be different than a brand selling protein supplements.

We need completely different welcome flows.

Um, use this discount code. Ends tonight. Yada, yada, yada. Shop now.

Super simple. This is a super simple flow, but it is going to do well for you.

But keep in mind, again, people are receiving campaigns and most of your retention and repeat purchases are going to come from those.

Campaigns since people are receiving them three times per week.

So make sure you keep that in mind, but that's the win back flow.

It's always nice to have this in place.

Um, just in case you forget to send campaigns for like two weeks, which hopefully that never happens to you.

But if you ever, um, aren't able to send some campaigns, then you're going to have these firing out and getting purchases for people.

So that's what we want to do.

# **Intro To Flow Strategy**

# **What Are Email Flows?**

Flows are the automated emails that get sent to a customer after they complete a specified action.  
Things like joining the email list, abandoning an item they added to cart, or making a purchase.  
Flows allow us to automate the customer journey and send high converting reminders, cross sells, and relevant information to the customer.  
They should make up around 50% of your total email revenue with the other 50% coming from campaigns.

* Automated revenue ✅  
* Take pressure off weekly campaigns ✅  
* Convert high intent traffic ✅  
* Recover lost sales (abandonment flows) ✅  
* Personalize customer experience ✅

## **Already Have Flows?**

Maybe you have base Email Flows live (automated emails) such as the Welcome Flow, Checkout Abandon Flow, Post Purchase Flow, and maybe a few others.  
They are likely only a **few emails long**. Maybe 3-4 sometimes less, sometimes more.  
*But here’s the real kicker*:  
The designs and copy on your flows probably haven’t been updated since we were required to wear masks outside…  
Flows need to be updated with the times, trends, and optimized as the brand and customer changes.

# **Welcome Flow**

## **The CAC Problem Fix: Pop-Up \+ Welcome Flow**

You can’t lower ad costs…

But you *can* make every click worth more.

A dialed-in pop-up turns paid traffic into owned traffic. And a powerful welcome flow turns curiosity into conversions.

It’s the cheapest, highest-leverage way to stretch your ad dollars.

Instead of paying $50 to *maybe* get a sale… You pay $50, get the email, build trust, and convert them now, or later, without paying again.

That’s how you beat rising CAC.

⚠️ **What Happens If You Don’t Nail This?**  
→ Missed trust window → abandoned subscribers → future purchases lost forever.

## **Welcome Flow Non-Negotiables**

Every welcome flow will be a little different.  
But there are just a couple of high leveraged things we need to make sure our welcome flow includes.

* Turn off double opt-in  
* First email fires immediately upon sign-up  
* At least 3 emails long  
* Emails 1-2 days apart  
* Remind of welcome discount in every email  
* Include a text based email  
* Include a last chance discount email

## **Creating Your Strategy**

Since every brand is different, every welcome flow strategy should be different as well.  
Some welcome flows can we 3-4 emails others should be 6-8 emails.  
We need to look into the brand and think of what the customer may be thinking…

* What is important to them?  
* What are their objections?  
* Is buying your product an impulse purchase or a decision that takes time to decide?

**Base Strategy:**

## **Welcome Flow Template**

### **Email \#1: Welcome Email \+ Deliver Incentive**

The first email should be pretty similar across brands in terms of layout. Deliver your incentive and give some brand background.

### **1-5 Filler Emails**

Some brands need more education than others. You can plug any filler emails with relevant information as long as you remind of the discount in every one of them.

### **Last Chance Discount Email**

Use heavy urgency to let someone know that their discount is disappearing.

### **Text Based Support Email**

Reach out from the founder or support team and offer support and a final push for the discount.

## **Welcome Email \#1**

**Purpose and Content**

* Deliver a strong intro on your brand in the briefest way possible while giving them what they want, the discount.  
* People are really only here for the discount (or freebie) and maybe get a quick summary of what your brand has to offer before they continue shopping.  
* We want the design to simple, clean, and easy to follow.  
* The goal is simply to get people to click to browse onto the site.

**Email Template**

* **Hero section** with discount code, button, and brief intro.  
* **Bridge section** with quick brand summary  
* **Product section** with products / categories

## **Insert 1-4 Filler Emails**

**What Are Filler Emails (And Why Do They Matter)?**  
Filler emails are the 1–5 educational emails sent between your first “Welcome” email and your “Last Chance” email.  
People need education and more than a couple reminders to buy.  
We want to stay top of mind as they may be checking out other competitors.  
**Why Include Them:**  
✅ **Build Trust** – Most customers won’t buy immediately. These emails bridge the gap.  
✅ **Overcome Objections** – Answer unspoken questions that stop people from converting.  
✅ **Position Your Brand** – Show what makes you different, how your product works, and why it’s worth the price.  
✅ **Prime Future Buyers** – Even if they don’t buy now, you’ve laid the groundwork for later.

### **Other Filler Email Examples:**

* **One Benefit Feature:** Pick one benefit from your product and make a whole email just talking about that one thing.  
* **One Feature:** Pick one feature from your product such as it's ingredients or materials and make a whole email just talking about that one thing.  
* **How It's Made:** Go through the process of how your item is made, it's interesting and builds trust with the customer.  
* **How To Use:** Show the customer how to get the most out of your product with instructions and tips.  
* **Trending:** Highlight products that are selling well in the season or month you are in.  
* **Testimonials:** Highlight 1-5 reviews that are on your site to boost credibility.  
* **Us vs Them:** Compare your product and how it compares to other options on the market.  
* **Myths vs Facts:** Overcome certain beliefs in your niche and position your product as a solution.  
* **Blog Promo:** Highlight a blog that is on your site and drive traffic to it.  
* **Research Study Highlight:** Find a research study online that is related to your product and it's ingredients / material to prove that it is effective.  
* **Customer Transformation**: Highlight real stories of how your product or service has positively impacted or transformed a customer's life or business.  
* **Before vs After**: Showcase the effectiveness of your product or service through compelling before-and-after comparisons.  
* **Note From The Founder**: Provide a personal message from the founder or CEO, sharing insights, company news, or a personal touchpoint with subscribers.  
* **Gift Guide**: Curate a list of recommended products or services for various occasions, helping subscribers find the perfect gift.  
* **Treat Yourself**: Encourage self-care and indulgence with products or services designed to pamper or treat the subscriber.  
* **Media Publications**: Compile mentions of your company in the media, highlighting press coverage, interviews, or articles.  
* **Behind The Scenes**: Offer a glimpse into the inner workings of your company, showcasing the people, processes, or stories behind your products.  
* **Tips and Tricks**: Provide valuable advice, hacks, or strategies to help subscribers get more out of your products or improve their lives in some way.  
* **UGC Content**: Feature user-generated content, such as customer photos, reviews, or stories, to build community and authenticity.  
* **Staff Picks**: Highlight favorite products, books, or tools selected by team members, offering a personalized recommendation to subscribers.  
* **Brand Values**: Communicate the core values and mission of your brand, reinforcing your commitment to certain principles and building trust with your audience.

## **Last Chance Email**

**Purpose and Content**

* By the end of the flow, you should have trust built up and people just need a nudge to buy.  
* Drive urgency before a discount expires or a product sells out.  
* This is your final push to convert subscribers who’ve been on the fence.

**Email Template**

* **Hero section** that states the urgency: *“Last chance to use your code”* or *“Offer ends tonight”*  
* **Product section** tying in your brand and showcasing what they’ll regret missing out on (bestsellers, bundles, or products tied to the offer)

## **Text Based Support Email**

**Purpose and Content**

* This email combines a customer support tone with urgency to drive action.  
* It’s meant to feel like a friendly nudge — not a sales push.  
* Be sure to offer support and mention your support team.

**Email Template**

* Remind of the brand and it's mission  
* Mention the discount code and it is still active for use  
* Include a blurb about your support team available if the customer replies to the email

# **Site Abandon Flow**

## **What Is The Site Abandon Flow?**

This flow is going to trigger when someone goes to your website, but doesn’t actually view any products or go further down the funnel.  
Usually it’s because they didn’t find a product they like and we just need to show them what we have.  
The trigger to find inside of Klaviyo: **Active on Site**

## **Site Abandon Flow Strategy**

These users didn't click onto any products, so we want to show them what we have.  
We don't need too much here, especially since it is lower intent than a cart or browse abandon flow…  
Just 1 or 2 emails to re-engage them is perfect.

## **Site Abandon Email 1**

**Email Content:**

* Encourage browsing  
* Show product(s)  
* Offer support

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Find what you're looking for?  
* There's more here\!  
* We saw you seeing us…  
* Ready to come back?

Quick Tips:

* Include a button above the fold so the customer doesn't need to scroll  
* Make sure to include individual buttons for each product you should  
* Include category buttons for customers to shop by category (in the footer is fine)

**Email Example:**

## **Site Abandon Email 2**

**Email Content:**

* Personal reminder  
* Address brand uniqueness  
* Point in the right direction

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Just checking in  
* Find everything okay?  
* Here to help out

Quick Tips:

* Keep it short and snappy, no one wants to read an essay  
* Update the sender name to the founders name for a more personal feel

**Email Example:**

# **Browse Abandon Flow**

## **What Is The Abandoned Browse Flow?**

This flow triggers when someone views a product on your site but doesn’t add anything to their cart.  
It means they showed interest but didn’t take action—so our job is to remind them what caught their eye and guide them closer to purchase.  
The trigger to use in Klaviyo: **Viewed Product**

## **Abandoned Browse Flow Strategy**

These users *did* click on a product, which means they had intent—we just need to nudge them.  
This is higher intent than site abandon, so we want to be a bit more persuasive. Highlight benefits, provide social proof, and answer questions they might have.  
Also, the item they browsed may not be the one they want… so we want to show other products.  
**4 emails works well here**, spaced out over 3-4 days.

## **Abandoned Browse Email 1**

**Email Content:**

* Dynamic Klaviyo block  
* Give social proof  
* Show other products

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Something catch your eye?  
* Watcha got there?  
* Saved for you

Quick Tips:

* Make your buttons nice and big  
* Feature multiple products that may better fit the customer  
* Put the dynamic content block as high up in the email as possible

**Email Example:**

## **Abandoned Browse Email 2**

**Email Content:**

* Dynamic Klaviyo block  
* Simple reminder  
* Answer FAQs

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Take another look\!  
* Quick reminder  
* Don't wait on this one

Quick Tips:

* Answer only 3 FAQs to avoid overwhelm  
* Try to sneak in some social proof (one testimonial is good)

**Email Example:**

## **Abandoned Browse Email 3**

**Email Content:**

* Discount opener  
* Dynamic Klaviyo block  
* Show other products

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Still thinking?  
* A gift for you 🎁  
* 10% OFF your viewed item\!

Quick Tips:

* Mention the discount in the preview text or subject line  
* Put the discount high up in the email for easy access

**Email Example:**

## **Abandoned Browse Email 4**

**Email Content:**

* Text-based last chance  
* Personal discount reminder  
* Educate on brand info

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Checking in on your gift  
* Hey, It's Ashley\!  
* Saved this discount for you…

Quick Tips:

* Bold your main points, especially the discount  
* Make it seem personal, like the founder reaching out

**Email Example:**

## **Browse Abandon Dynamic Content Formulas**

* Create a "Table" block in Klaviyo  
* Add an image on the left side  
* Use a "Dynamic Image"  
* Insert the dynamic variable "{{ event.ImageURL }}"  
* Add text on the right side  
* For the item name use "{{ event.Name }}"  
* For the price use the variable "{{ event.Price|default:'' }}"  
* Add the correct link to the photo, text, and buttons  
* Use the dynamic link "{{ event.URL }}" which will take the customer top the product page they viewed

# **Cart / Checkout Abandon Flows**

## **What Is The Abandoned Cart Flow?**

This flow triggers when someone adds a product to their cart but doesn’t start the checkout process.  
They liked what they saw *enough to add it to cart*, so this is high intent—we just need to push them across the finish line.  
It isn't the farthest intent where they go to the payment page (checkout abandon), but it is still very high intent.  
The trigger to use in Klaviyo: **Added to Cart**

## **What Is The Abandoned Checkout Flow?**

This flow triggers when someone starts the checkout process (enters email/starts filling out info) but doesn’t complete the purchase.  
This is *the highest* intent flow—they were right there and just didn’t finish.  
The trigger to use in Klaviyo: **Started Checkout**

## **Abandoned Cart / Checkout Flows Strategy**

These users are *one or two clicks away*—so every email should reduce friction, provide assurance, and make buying feel easy.  
To save time, you can use the same emails for both of these flows. If you have time, try to make them slightly different.  
We really want to mix in an emotional feel, we've found that text based emails from the founder perform really well with getting people over the edge. 

## **Abandoned Cart / Checkout Email 1**

**Email Content:**

* Simple reminder  
* Dynamic Klaviyo block  
* Brief product Info

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Your order is ready to ship  
* One click away\!  
* Your cart is waiting

Quick Tips:

* Include relevant shipping information (should as free shipping threshold)  
* Have the button and dynamic content at the very top of the email so the customer doesn't need to scroll

**Email Example:**

## **Abandoned Cart / Checkout Email 2**

**Email Content:**

* Text-based email  
* Personal reminder  
* Social proof

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Quick check-in  
* Holding onto your order  
* Have any questions with your order?

Quick Tips:

* List out just a couple social proof points  
* Avoid using too many CTAs, keep it simple

**Email Example:**

## **Abandoned Cart / Checkout Email 3**

**Email Content:**

* Discount opener  
* Dynamic Klaviyo block  
* Social proof / FAQs

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* $XX OFF Your Cart\!  
* Limited time discount on your order  
* Just for you 🎁

Quick Tips:

* Feature the discount code and offer at the very top of the email  
* Keep it as simple as possible… the discount should be the main aspect of the email

**Email Example:**

## **Abandoned Cart / Checkout Email 4**

**Email Content:**

* Text-based last chance  
* Personal discount reminder  
* Offer support

**Subject Line Ideas:**

* Last chance  
* Still want XX% OFF?  
* Your cart is expiring (and your gift)

Quick Tips:

* Add a PS section offering support  
* Bold your main points and discounts for the customer  
* Avoid too many CTAs that will cause overwhelm

**Email Example:**

## **Cart Abandon Dynamic Content**

* Create a "Split" block in Klaviyo  
* Add an image on the left side  
* Use a "Dynamic Image"  
* Insert the dynamic variable "{{ event.ImageURL }}"  
* Add text on the right side  
* For the item name use "{{ event.Name }}"  
* For the price use the variable "{{ event.Price|default:'' }}"  
* Add the correct checkout link to the photo, text, and buttons  
* If you have a cart page on your site, use that link.  
* Will most likely be "storename.com/cart"  
* If you don't, use the dynamic link "{{ event.URL }}"

## **Checkout Abandon Dynamic Content**

**Abandoned Checkout Dynamic Content**

* Create a "Table" block in Klaviyo  
* Under table settings select "Dynamic"  
* Under "Row collection" enter "event.extra.line\_items"  
* Under "Row alias" enter "item"  
* Add an image on the left side  
* Use a "Dynamic Image"  
* Insert the dynamic variable "{% if item.product.variant.images.0.src %}{{item.product.variant.images.0.src}}{%else%}{{item.product.images.0.src|missing\_product\_image}}{%endif%}"  
* Add text on the right side  
* For the item name use "{{ item.product.title }}"  
* For the price use the variable "{% currency\_format item.line\_price|floatformat:2 %}"  
* For the quantity use the variable "{{ item.quantity|floatformat:0 }}"  
* Add the correct checkout link to the photo, text, and buttons  
* Use the dynamic variable "{{ event.extra.checkout\_url|default:'' }}"

# **Post-Purchase Flow**

## **What Is The Post-Purchase Flow?**

This flow triggers when someone places an order with you.  
The trigger to use in Klaviyo: **Placed Order**

## **Post-Purchase Flow Strategy**

Let’s think about the customer and what they’re feeling right after they make a purchase…

* They have the propensity to buy… and are obviously in the mood for it since they just placed an order  
* They may be feeling excited for their order  
* They may have some uncertainty on their product or order

We want to address all of these in our post purchase flow to improve the customer experience and potentially get more sales.

# **Post-Purchase Email 1**

**Purpose & Content**

* Create a personalized customer experience right after purchase  
* Strengthen brand connection by thanking them and sharing mission  
* Encourage another purchase while they’re in a high buying window  
* Pro tip: Split by \# of products purchased → adjust message for authenticity

**Email Template**

* **Hero section**: Sincere thank-you from the founder (warm, personal tone)  
* **Body copy**: Reinforce brand mission \+ highlight their impact (“you’re contributing to something bigger”)  
* **Support note**: Offer help or next steps (FAQs, contact info, onboarding)  
* **CTA**: Gentle push to shop again (tailored reccs or upsell)

**Email Example:**

# **Post-Purchase Email 2**

**Purpose & Content**

* Improve customer experience \+ product enjoyment  
* Reduce returns by ensuring customers know how to use their product  
* Build trust by showing you care about their success  
* Pro tip: Split by product or category to give tailored instructions

**Email Template**

* **Hero section**: Excited headline about their order (“Your \[Product\] is here, let’s make the most of it\!”)  
* **Step-by-step section**: Clear instructions on setup or usage  
* **Tips & tricks**: Extra guidance or hacks for best results  
* **CTA**: Encourage ongoing use or explore related products

**Email Example:**

# **Feel Free To Add More Emails\!**

This strategy is just a simple starting point. You know your brand better than anyone.  
You're welcome to add more emails here if you think it would be relevant in the first 14 days after a customer purchase.  
Here are some ideas:

**Order On The Way**

Cross-Sell

**Social Media Push**

**Founder Check-In**

# **Replenishment Reminder Flow**

## **What Is The Replenishment Reminder Flow?**

The Replenishment Reminder Flow is an automated email sequence that reminds customers to reorder products they’re likely running low on.  
This can be exclusive for CPG brands, but if you don't have a CPG brand you're welcome to include this.  
**Why it Matters**

* Keeps customers stocked on their favorite products  
* Reduces churn by preventing “I forgot to buy” drop-offs  
* Drives repeat purchases without heavy discounts

**Example Use Cases**

* Protein powder running out after 30 servings  
* Coffee beans lasting \~2 weeks  
* Skincare bottles lasting 60 days

## **Replenishment Reminder Flow Strategy**

The replenishment reminder strategy is simple.  
We send two emails. The first goes out on the expected reorder date with a friendly nudge that it’s time to restock. The second goes out seven days later if they haven’t reordered, using a flash discount to create urgency and push the conversion.  
The first email leans on timing and convenience while the second adds incentive for those still on the fence.  
No need to get out of line here… as these users will still be receiving campaign emails and will rebuy from those.

# **Replenishment Reminder Email 1**

**Purpose & Content**

* Remind customers it’s time to restock before they run out  
* Position restocking as staying prepared and confident (no “caught off guard” moments)  
* Encourage an easy re-purchase with a one-click CTA  
* Optionally upsell with bundles or second product suggestions

**Email Template**

* **Hero section**: Bold urgency headline (“Time for a Fresh Breath Restock\!”)  
* **Body copy**: Friendly reminder it’s been a while since their last order, nudge to reorder now  
* **Value props section**: Highlight convenience (pocket-sized, 3-sec refresh, lasts 6+ hours, scientifically proven)  
* **Product showcase**: Flavor options \+ bundles to encourage variety or higher order value  
* **CTA**: Strong, clear button (“Refresh Your Breath” / “Shop Now”)

# **Replenishment Reminder Email 2**

**Purpose & Content**

* Create urgency for customers who haven’t reordered after the first reminder  
* Incentivize action with a limited-time discount code  
* Frame the message around convenience (“don’t get caught without it”) and lifestyle use cases  
* Position the offer as short-lived to increase FOMO

**Email Template**

* **Hero section**: Discount-focused headline (“15% Off Your Next Refill\!”)  
* **Body copy**: Reminder to restock \+ highlight pain point of running out unexpectedly  
* **Offer section**: Show discount clearly with code (e.g., COOL15)  
* **CTA**: Urgent button (“Refill Now”) paired with a reminder that the deal won’t last

# **Winback Flow**

## **What Is The Winback Flow?**

A Winback Flow is an automated email sequence sent to past customers who haven’t purchased in a while.  
**Why it Matters**

* Re-engages lapsed customers  
* Prevents churn and boosts retention  
* Reminds them why they loved your brand

**How it Works**

* Triggered after a SEGMENT you create, setting a period of someone not reordering after a past purchase (90, 120, 180 days)  
* Starts with a friendly “we miss you” reminder  
* Progresses to stronger incentives (special offer, discount, or exclusive product access)

**Segment Definition for 90 Day Winback Flow:**

## **Winback Flow Strategy**

The winback flow is designed to re-engage past customers who haven’t purchased in a while. These customers already know your brand, but they need a reminder of why they loved it in the first place.  
The sequence is simple but effective:  
**Email 1 (Day 0):** A warm “We miss you” message to reconnect and spark familiarity. The goal here is emotional—remind them of the positive experience they had with your brand.  
**Email 2 (Day 7):** If they don’t respond, introduce a discount (X% off). This adds a tangible reason to return and lowers the barrier to purchase.  
**Email 3 (Day 10):** A final “Last Chance” discount to drive urgency. This is the last push before considering them inactive, so the tone should be direct and urgent.  
This flow works because it moves from emotional connection → financial incentive → urgency, giving you three chances to bring a lapsed customer back before they churn completely.

# **Winback Email 1**

**Purpose & Content**

* Reconnect with lapsed customers by showing warmth and familiarity  
* Remind them of the experience your product creates (cocktail enjoyment, lifestyle tie-in)  
* Encourage them to check their supplies and consider restocking  
* Light, non-salesy tone to re-establish relationship

**Email Template**

* **Hero section**: Headline about restocking essentials (“Time to Restock Your Bar”)  
* **Body copy**: Friendly reminder they may be running low, highlight the fun and creativity of using your product  
* **Content add-ons**: Recipes, social proof, or community engagement prompts  
* **CTA**: Invite them back with an easy button (“Get Back Into It”)

# **Winback Email 2**

**Purpose & Content**

* Introduce a financial incentive for customers who didn’t respond to the first nudge  
* Make the message about appreciation (“we miss you”) while offering value  
* Highlight essential products and easy replacements to drive quick conversions

**Email Template**

* **Hero section**: Clear discount headline (“Take 10% Off Your Next Order”)  
* **Body copy**: Friendly explanation with discount code  
* **Product showcase**: Display replacement items or essentials to make reordering easy  
* **CTA**: Strong button driving urgency to shop (“Shop Now”)

# **Winback Email 3**

**Purpose & Content**

* Final push to convert lapsed customers  
* Use urgency-driven language (last chance, ends tonight)  
* Re-emphasize lifestyle benefit and remind them not to miss out  
* Position as a true final opportunity before flow ends

**Email Template**

* **Hero section**: Urgent discount headline (“Last Chance for 10% Off”)  
* **Body copy**: Remind them of the value and reinforce FOMO (running out, missing the deal)  
* **Offer section**: Discount code clearly displayed with expiration  
* **CTA**: Direct, urgent button (“Shop Now”)

# **Flow Optimization**

## **The Cons of Flow Optimization & A/B Testing**

Considering you can have 30-50+ automated emails…

* It's overwhelming  
* Hard to monitor at scale  
* Difficult to track and report at scale  
* Documenting is difficult

## **Long-Term Optimization Strategy**

Rather than have a ton of active tests in flows of all sorts of different triggers, trying out different content, etc  
We mostly use campaigns as our testing ground.  
Try new angles and A/B test frequently in your email blasts.  
Rather than waiting for results like you have to do in flows, you can get near instant data with campaigns.  
We literally put full campaigns that perform well into our flows.  
Go back and edit your flow emails across all triggers to include the angle you saw work.  
If it's an an educational email on the product creation process, we'll add it to the Welcome Flow.  
If it's an email about how to get the most out of your product, add it to your Post Purchase Flow.  
If it's an email that goes over your return policy, add it to your cart abandon flow to reverse risk.  
Don't reinvent the wheel\!

# **Flow Specific A/B Tests**

## **Flow Time Delays**

This is the biggest lever I'd say.  
Testing the amount of time from the action of the customer and the first email they receive.  
Then also, the time delay between your flow messages.

## **Long Form vs Short Form**

Especially for flows, we want to test longer form content vs shorter form.  
This will also apply to campaigns but especially in flows like abandonments when people have objections.  
Sometimes it makes sense to be quick and get an impulse purchase, other times it makes sense to spend time working through objections.

## **SLs and PTs**

This is the obvious one, but hard to take the learnings and apply to future emails since there will be a lot of variation.  
Here are some tests you can run:

* Including discount vs not including (for sales)  
* Including or excluding emojis  
* Ilusing "…" or exlcuding  
* Adding time delays  
* Using all caps vs not

## **Graphic vs Text Based**

Heavy text based emails vs graphic based emails can really vary across accounts.  
Test out different styles to see what your list responds to better.  
Especially for big drops and sales…  
We find that you want to mix them up but use text based for key emails.  
Example of Text Based sale email winner:

## **Promoting Categories vs Products**

We've noticed that some brand customers prefer to shop in their emails by category and some by individual products.  
Important to mix it up but you can bias one option more, especially for important emails, if it performs better.  
Example of categories performing better:

# **That's It For This One\!**

Thanks for reading, I hope you got some value :)

# **Want More Help?**

If you're an ecommerce brand doing at least $50k/mo \- book a free discovery consultation call.  
Ecommerce Email Flow Mastery

# CAMPAIGNS

# Campaign Strategy

* Campaigns are your one-off sends at a specific date & Time  
* Send between 2x \- 4x per week   
* Content: Stay TOP of mind  
  * Who are you going to call when your power goes out? The electrician who's knocking at your door  
* Send frequency will vary based on different factors, primarily on store revenue & site traffic  
* Find a good balance of Graphic & Text-Based Emails  
* Educate the customer on a problem, position yourself as the solution. Don't throw around discounts like candy... Dilutes & cheapens the brand

# Campaign Calendar Creation

* **Highest Lever \-- Your Ideas**  
* **Content Pillars**  
  * **Educational** (ex. FAQs/ingredient education)  
  * **Social Proof** (ex. customer testimonials/media features)  
  * **Community / Branded** (Ex. brand story/founder messages)  
  * **Product or Collection Highlights** (ex. trending products/gift guides)  
  * **Sales** (ex. limited discounts)   
* **Resources for high-concentration calendars**  
  * [**Milled**](https://milled.com/)   
  * [**Email Donut**](https://www.emaildonut.com/)  
  * [**Really Good Emails**](https://reallygoodemails.com/)

# Creating Great Campaigns

* In a world of instant dopamine, content is king  
* S.C.E Framework  
* Use Infographics to break up complex information & break up big text blocks

# Segmentation

* Dynamic customer cohorts (groups) determined by meeting certain conditions  
  * (Placed order in the last 30 days, opened email in the last 90 days, etc.)  
* **Importance of Segmentation**  
  * Better deliverability  
  * More sales  
  * Set up flows  
  * Personalization  
* Don't overthink it... Your 90-day engaged list is going to be your bread and butter, accounting for 80%-90% of your sales & engagement  
* Exclusion segments should include (but not be limited to)  
  * Bounced 3+ times

Link to document in video: [https://gamma.app/docs/Email-Marketing-Campaigns-uefllw8w707qptn](https://gamma.app/docs/Email-Marketing-Campaigns-uefllw8w707qptn)

# Text Based Email Masterclass

See what makes a great text based email, why they're effective, and how to build a great one yourself\!

Plain text emails are a **highly effective, underrated e-commerce marketing strategy** that can generate significant revenue. These emails, **without any design or graphics**, stand out and connect with customers on a personal level.

**Key Pointers for Success:**

* **Why they work:**  
  * **Stand out** from graphic-heavy emails (0:47).  
  * Feel **personal**, like a friend sent them (1:02).  
  * Build **trust** by appearing human, not corporate (2:47).  
* **Best practices:**  
  * Keep it **short**: under 250 words (6:43).  
  * Use **1-2 clear calls to action** (7:00).  
  * Send from a **real person** (or created persona) (7:25).  
  * Include a **"PS" section** for key messages (7:37).  
* **When to use them:**  
  * In **key email flows** (welcome, abandonment, post-purchase, win-back) (13:08).  
  * Mix with graphic emails; aim for a **4:1 graphic to plain text ratio** (13:18).  
  * Send at least **two plain text emails per month** (one educational, one promotional) (13:26).  
  * **Test different senders** and content (13:37).

# GAMMA CAMPAIGNS

Email Marketing Campaigns

# **Email Marketing Campaigns**

# **Campaigns Strategy**

# **What Are Campaigns?**

Campaigns are the one-time emails you send to your list at a specific date and time.  
It's the easiest way in ecommerce to get consistent, cheap traffic from customers who already know, like, and trust you.  
We need a consistent cadence of 2-4 email campaigns per week to establish a relationship with the consumer where they get used to seeing you in the inbox and in the habit of opening your emails.  
If you're sending good content that people enjoy such as tips, education, etc then people will look forward to your emails.

## **How Many Campaigns Should You Send?**

Every brand is different and of course it depends.  
3x per week is typically the sweet spot for retaining your list while also getting good consistent revenue.  
I wouldn’t recommend going lower than 2x per week no matter your ecom store size.

**Sending 1-2x Per Week**

* Customer forgets about you  
* Customers unable to form a habit of opening emails  
* Leaving money on the table

**Sending 5-7x Per Week**

* Higher unsubscribes  
* Customers can get annoyed  
* Dilutes the power of your messaging  
* Revenue plateaus

**Sending 2-4x Per Week**

* Higher revenue  
* Low unsubscribes  
* Stay top of mind with customer  
* Every email you send has equal weight and impact

# **The Key For Scaling Profitably**

Content is also a key part in **staying top of mind with your past customers** to promote repeat purchases.  
If you're in someone's face 2x-4x per week they have no choice but to keep you in their mind.  
Even if they don't open all of our emails, they see our name.  
This is what we want. **Occupy mental real estate**.  
Whenever your customer is going about their day cleaning the kitchen and have a random need come up for your product, YOU will be the immediate brand they turn to.

## **Sending Rule of Thumb**

Every brand is different…  
But typically this is a good  rule of thumb for email campaign frequency.  
If your store revenue OR monthly site visitors fall into one category, use the following frequency.  
(We use monthly site visitors as a metric too because this will impact list growth and new subscribers coming in, we can send more emails and risk more unsubscribes because we have new people consistently coming to the list through our pop-up form which hopefully you are optimizing)

| Store Revenue | Monthly Site Visitors | Email Frequency |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| $0-50k/mo | 0-25k/mo | 2x per week |
| $50k-250k/mo | 25k-50k/mo | 3x per week |
| $250k-1M/mo | 50k-250k/mo | 4x per week |
| $1M/mo+ | 250k/mo+ | 5-6x per week |

## **Mixing Graphic & Text-Based Emails**

We want to use a balance of graphic and text-based emails.  
**Graphic based emails** are needed for branding and showing off your products.  
They are also needed for list engagement because we need to visually appeal our customers to stay subscribed (we can’t send the same stuff over and over again, the list will get bored and unsubscribe).  
**Text-based emails** are the secret sauce in today's ecom world.  
They are plain text emails that look like they're coming from a friend in your inbox. These convert so well because they add a personal touch and are simple and easy to click.  
We want mostly graphic based emails to keep the list engaged visually, but then strategically mix in text-based emails for a few sends of the month to give that personal feel people will convert from.

**Graphic Based Email**

**Text-Based Email**

# **Education \+ Positioning \= Sales Without Discounts**

The modern customer wants to do their due diligence and understand the product they are buying.  
We want to remove as much friction as possible for them in that process.  
The content that wins will do two things:  
**A) Educate the customer on a problem, a process, or a solution**  
**B) Position your product as the solution**  
If you can do both, you will guide your customer to making the right decision.  
And earn trust points in the process needed to make a buying decision.

# 

# **Building Campaign Calendars**

# **Your Ideas Are The Highest Lever**

Calendar and idea creation is the most important process to email marketing.  
You can't just throw together "bestseller" emails and hope you'll get sales.  
You need to find unique angles, fun ideas, and seasonal campaigns that people actually care about.  
The idea behind your email \> The actual email

## **What Should You Be Sending?**

We have a few different content pillars we want to send emails for…

* **Educational**  (ex. FAQs / ingredient education)  
* **Social Proof** (ex. customer testimonials / media features)  
* **Community / Branded**  (Ex. brand story / founder messages)  
* **Product or Collection Highlights** (ex. trending products / gift guides)  
* **Sales** (ex. limited discounts)

We find a good cadence is having an even amount of emails for these 5 pillars.  
For example, if you send 10 emails a month; you could have 2 educational emails, 2 social proof emails, 2 product specific promos, 2 community / branded content emails , then 1 sale that takes 2 emails.

## **100 High-Converting Email Ideas**

### 📚 **Educational (FAQs, Ingredient Education, Product Use, Myths)**

* **Top 5 FAQs, Answered** – Tackle objections head-on.  
* **Ingredient Highlight: \[X\]** – Why it’s in your product and what it does.  
* **How It’s Made** – Give a behind-the-scenes look into sourcing or production.  
* **Label Decoder** – Teach how to read your packaging (certifications, materials, etc).  
* **You’ve Been Using It Wrong** – Demo better usage techniques.  
* **Before You Buy: What You Need to Know** – Educate them on what to expect.  
* **The \#1 Mistake Most Customers Make** – Fixable mistake and your product as the solution.  
* **What Makes Our Formula Different** – Go deep on your unique formulation or design.  
* **Break the Rules** – Dispel common myths or misconceptions.  
* **Why Quality Ingredients \= Better Results** – Contrast high quality vs cheap options.  
* **What’s NOT in Our Product** – Address concerns by omission.  
* **What Happens If You Stop Using It?** – Teach sustainability or long-term effects.  
* **How to Use \[Product\] In Your Daily Routine** – AM/PM or seasonal how-to.  
* **Science Behind Our Product** – Citing real research or proven outcomes.  
* **Better for You, Here’s Why** – Educational but conversion-friendly.  
* **How to Layer With Other Products** – Usage compatibility education.  
* **Explained: \[Specific Benefit\]** – Focus on one transformation or mechanism.  
* **What We Wish Every Customer Knew** – Founder or expert tip list.  
* **Myths vs Facts: Industry Lies You’ve Been Told** – A hot-take style explainer.  
* **Step-by-Step Usage Guide** – Visual or checklist style email.

### 👥 **Social Proof (Testimonials, Reviews, UGC, Press)**

* **"I Was Skeptical Until..."** – A powerful review story.  
* **Before and After** – Transformation spotlight.  
* **Customer Story of the Month** – Real human, real results.  
* **Your Words, Not Ours** – Text-only review collage email.  
* **Video Review Highlight** – Feature a 30s customer clip.  
* **Fan Favorites According to You** – Bestsellers based on reviews.  
* **Your Voice Matters** – Ask for feedback while highlighting past reviews.  
* **Top Reviewed Products Right Now** – Star ratings and mini blurbs.  
* **Social Media Roundup** – Tag-based or influencer content.  
* **Rated 4.9 Stars… Here’s Why** – Review-based breakdown.  
* **95% of Customers Say…** – Use internal survey data.  
* **Most Unexpected Reviews** – Highlight unique use cases.  
* **What You Said, What We Did** – Product improvements based on feedback.  
* **As Seen In \[Media Outlet\]** – Subtle flex email.  
* **Real People. Real Results.** – Grid of mini testimonials with faces.  
* **Influencer Spotlight** – Subtle UGC from someone with authority.  
* **\#FanOfTheMonth** – Celebrate and reward a community member.  
* **Customer Poll Results** – Share outcome of IG/Email vote.  
* **This Product Changed My Life** – Long-form emotional review.  
* **Top Rated by Pet Parents / Moms / Athletes, etc.** – Segment-driven social proof.

### 🏠 **Community / Branded (Founder Messages, Mission, Team)**

* **A Note From the Founder** – Values, gratitude, or a personal insight.  
* **Why We Exist** – Share your "why" story.  
* **Brand Timeline: How We Got Here** – Visual journey email.  
* **Our Mission, In Your Words** – Share mission through customer stories.  
* **Meet the Team Behind the Magic** – Spotlight a few faces and fun facts.  
* **The Story Behind \[Product Name\]** – How it came to be.  
* **Culture Corner** – What the team’s reading, listening to, or vibing with.  
* **A Look Inside Launch Week** – BTS of your brand hustle.  
* **We’re Listening** – Feedback invite \+ transparency email.  
* **How We’re Giving Back** – Charitable partnerships or donations.  
* **Our Values** – Fun visual explainer.  
* **From Our Family to Yours** – Warm, humanizing message.  
* **Founder's Favorites** – What they use and love.  
* **We’re Hiring** – Invite referrals and show growth.  
* **Happy \[Brand\] Anniversary** – Reflection \+ thank you.  
* **What We Believe In** – Brand manifesto style.  
* **Packaging Evolution** – How you improved sustainability.  
* **How We Built This (with $X in the Bank)** – Transparent founder journey.  
* **Your Stories Inspire Us** – User-submitted content \+ appreciation.  
* **Our Vision for the Future** – Where your brand is going.

### 🔍 **Product or Collection Highlights (Spotlights, Bundles, Guides)**

* **Product Spotlight: \[Top SKU\]** – Deep-dive on one hero item.  
* **Trending Now** – What’s hot on your site this week.  
* **Staff Favorites** – Curated list with team picks.  
* **Just Landed: New Arrivals** – Fresh drops.  
* **This Pairing \=** 🔥 – Complementary product bundles.  
* **Bundle & Save (Without Discounts)** – Stackable non-sale values.  
* **Build Your Routine / Kit** – Step-by-step bundle builder.  
* **Your Wishlist, Delivered** – Based on browsing or season.  
* **Limited Edition Look** – Product with a short shelf life.  
* **What’s Back In Stock** – High demand \= urgency.  
* **Restock Alert: You Asked, We Listened** – Based on past demand.  
* **TSA-Approved / Travel-Friendly Picks** – Summer or travel focused.  
* **Back to School / Work / Gym Picks** – Life event themed.  
* **Pet-Friendly or Kid-Safe?** – Tailored highlight email.  
* **Gift Guide: For Her/Him/Them** – Occasions or roles.  
* **Under $50 / Budget-Friendly Bestsellers** – Low-commitment AOV items.  
* **Seasonal Must-Haves** – Fall, Winter, etc.  
* **Your Daily Essentials Kit** – Routine-builder spotlight.  
* **Best Sellers vs Hidden Gems** – Contrast feature.  
* **Editor's Picks** – High-end or aesthetic curation.

### 💸 **Sales (Discounts, Urgency, Special Offers)**

* **Teaser Email** – “Something big is coming…” founder text.  
* **Launch Day: Here’s the Offer** – Clean and clear CTA.  
* **How to Shop the Sale** – Guide-style breakdown.  
* **Back in Stock \= Sale** – Combine FOMO with scarcity.  
* **Final Hours: Ends Tonight** – Timer and urgency.  
* **VIP Early Access** – Segment-only release.  
* **Exclusive for Subscribers Only** – Email-only access.  
* **Weekend Only Flash Sale** – Tight deadline.  
* **BOGO or Bundle Sale** – No discount code needed.  
* **Birthday Sale** – Brand or subscriber birthday tie-in.  
* **Holiday Sale** – Traditional or quirky national days.  
* **Last Call: Final Few Units Left** – Inventory countdown.  
* **Sitewide Sale vs Product-Specific** – Highlight both.  
* **Stock Up & Save** – Tiered discount or AOV play.  
* **Gift With Purchase** – High perceived value promo.  
* **Refer a Friend \+ Save** – Referral email with dual benefit.  
* **Mystery Offer** – Scratch-off style or curiosity subject line.  
* **Your Favorites Are on Sale** – Personalized collection.  
* **Secret Sale (No Code Needed)** – Surprise access email.  
* **Sale Extended\!** – Save lagging sales with a bonus round.

# **Creating High-Converting Campaigns**

## **The 2025/2026 Customer**

The average customer today is scared of reading.  
With Tik Tok, Instagram, and Snapchat offering instant dopamine… how can you blame them?  
The average time you have with the customer's attention span has shrunk drastically in the last couple years.  
Before, you could have had an average of 5-10 seconds of attention per email.  
In 2025/2026, you *maybe* have 3 seconds.  
**Your ability to get somebody to convert is completely dependent on how fast and effectively you can get your point across.**

## **The S.C.E. Framework for Email Marketing**

We use a simple framework to write and design emails that sell:  
**S — Skimmable**  
No blocks of copy. Break up copy. Add line breaks. Use bolding and headlines. The modern customer has a TikTok brain, if they can’t skim it in 3 seconds, it won’t get read.  
**C — Clear & Concise**  
Focus on one core takeaway per email. Customers are in zombie mode in their inbox. Make your message obvious and easy to absorb.  
**E — Engaging**  
Boring emails don’t convert. Use education, humor, punchy phrasing, emotional triggers, or helpful info. Emails should give people a hit of dopamine.

**1\. Skimmable**  
The use of sections, quick copy, and bolded points allow this email to be skimmed in under 3 seconds.

**2\. Clear and Concise**  
This email is focused on one thing, not five, allowing the customer to easily comprehend.

**3\. Engaging**  
Fun and educational copy that is directly applied to the customer increases engagement.

## **Use Infographics\!**

The cheat code for email engagement is using infographics.  
Nobody wants to read blocks of copy… your copy just won't be read.  
Instead, use these infographics to help increase your engagement 👇

Email Copywriting
