# **Segmentation**

## **What Is Segmentation?**

Segments are very similar to lists, but different in one key way.  
**Lists are stagnant.** As the name suggests, it's just a flat list of people.  
**Segments are dynamic**. They can change at any second, based on the requirements you set on the segment.  
As the name suggests, it's a *segment* of your full email list. A subset of people who meet certain characteristics you provide.

## **Why Should You Segment Your Campaigns**

Segmentation allows you to pinpoint certain members of your list so that you can gather data and create targeted emails to send based on where they are at in the customer journey.  
Benefits of proper segmentation:

* Improved deliverability  
* More sales  
* Save time with automation  
* Personalized customer experience

## **Basic Segmentation Use Cases**

#### **\[Most Important\] Engaged Lists for Improved Deliverability**

You want to segment your list for campaigns to only include people who have recently opened or clicked emails so you ensure you only send to people who want to open your emails. If you send to people who aren't opening emails, Gmail will notice and start sending you to spam\!

#### **Interested Customers for More Sales**

You can target people who have been active on your site or even added items to their cart but haven't made a purchase and send them extra content to try to get them to cross the line.

#### **Targeted Campaigns Based on Product Interests**

Another possibility is sending emails about specific product collections to people who have bought or shown interest in these items before

#### **Remove Unengaged Profiles**

It's important to always track and suppress people who haven't been engaging with your emails since customers will naturally churn. Since they aren't opening your emails you want to remove them from your list so you aren't harming your deliverability.

## **Let's Not Get Too Complicated…**

Yes, you could hyper segment your list by their favorite color and if their belly button is an innie or an outie…  
And yes, that would probably increase your conversion rate if you send emails specific to them…  
**But at what cost?**  
Would you rather send 1-2 targeted campaigns per week to different email groups of 10,000 people OR 3-4 broader email campaigns per week to 50,000 people each?  
The latter will outperform 99 times out of 100\.  
Yes, you should segment based on engagement… but don't get carried away and distracted from the basics.

## **Key Segments And Use Cases**

These are really the only segments you should be worrying about.  
Don't be concerned with any other segments until you are doing $1M/mo or have very specific use cases.

| Segment Name | Segment Definition | Segment Use Case |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| **90 Day Engaged List** (You can use any time frame, 90 is recommended to start) | Someone can receive email marketing because person is subscribed**AND** Someone has Opened Email at least once in the last 90 days**OR** Someone has been Active on Site at least once in the last 90 days**OR** Someone has Placed An Order at least once in the last 90 days | This is your base segment for sending all your email campaigns to. We want to only send emails to our active subscribers so we keep high open rates and click rates. If we send to unengaged people they will hurt our engagement rates, Google will notice, and we will land more often in the spam folder. |
| **High-Potential Purchasers** | Someone can receive email marketing because person is subscribed**AND** Someone has placed order 0 times in the past 30 days**AND** Someone has been active on site at least once in the past 30 days | For sending 1x-2x extra emails per month (on top of engaged list sends) This list is a set of people who have been active on your site and have made it somewhere in the funnel but haven't made a purchase. You can send them more emails to try to get them over the edge and increase your sales. |
| **Winback Potential Customers** (Time frames will vary based on your store and how soon people typically come back) | Someone can receive email marketing because person is subscribed**AND** Someone has placed order at least once in the past 150 days**AND** Someone has placed order zero times in the last 90 days | For sending 1x extra email per month (on top of engaged list sends) OR triggering into a Winback email flow. This list is exclusive to people who have bought a certain period of time ago but haven't purchased recently. The amount of time is going to vary based on the store and the average buying period in between purchases. For this group you will want a few flow emails to fire when they enter the segment encouraging them to come back and make another purchase. It may be a good idea to send them a discount as well. |
| **VIP Customers** (This will again vary on store, use your gut on what counts as a VIP customer) | Someone can receive email marketing because person is subscribed**AND** Someone has placed order at least 4 times over all time | For sending 1x extra emails per month (on top of engaged list sends) AND/OR triggering into an email flow This segment is pretty self explanatory. These are your customers who are fans of your brand and keep coming back. You can choose to make your VIP list either on their total number or purchases with you or total amount spent at your store. I prefer total number of purchases because you can predict it a little better and send emails to people every purchase letting them know how many purchases they are away from the VIP list. It's a good idea to have a simple VIP email flow to anybody who joins this segment thanking them, giving some exclusive content, and giving a generous offer. On top of this, it may be smart to send a VIP email once a quarter with an exclusive deal or offer. |
| **Interested in X Product / Category** (You can replace "category" in this segment with "product" if you choose) | Someone can receive email marketing because person is subscribed**AND** Someone has viewed item where category is \[category\] at least once over all time. **OR** Someone has added item to cart where category is \[category\] at least once over all time. **OR** Someone has started checkout with item where collection is \[collection\] at least once over all time. **OR** Someone has placed order with item where collection is \[collection\] at least once over all time. | This is a list of people who have had any interest in a specific product / category in your store that you set. This list can be great to send targeted sales, announcements, or releases related to the item(s) you have. You can do this for both products or specific categories, you just have to customize the filter on the segment definition to choose either "product" or "category". |
| **Suppress List** | Someone can receive email marketing because person is subscribed**AND** Someone has received email at least 5 times over all time**AND** Someone has opened email zero times in the last 365 days**OR** Someone has bounced email at least 3 times over all time**OR** Someone has marked email as spam at least once over all time | For not sending ANY emails to This is an email segment of people that aren't engaging with your emails and / or are high risk email profiles that don't deserve to be sent emails. You should be looking at this once a month and suppressing these profiles to save money on your Klaviyo bill, which will charge based on your email profiles. |

## **Segmentation Tip**

Just send email campaigns to your 90 Day Engaged List…  
We achieve these results just sending to our engaged list for 90% of sends.  
Email Marketing Campaigns

# COPYWRITING

# The Principles of Good Copy

* Clarity: Copy should be ultra easy to understand, with simple language and a clear message.  
* You have \~3 seconds to hook your reader. Eliminate fluff.   
* S.C.E Framework  
  * Skimmable  
  * Clear & Concise  
  * Engaging  
* Introduce Infographics to break up long blocks of text  
* 1 Main idea / Takeaway per email. The more ideas you introduce, the less the reader takes away

# ChatGPT Copywriting

* **AI isn't perfect, but capitalize on Chat GPT for the ideation, guides, frameworks**  
* **Any LLM is an iterative process. Embrace the back and forth.**   
* [**Email Marketing Brain \>\>\>**](https://wellcopy.net/gpt)  
* **Your prompts are everything. Lazy prompts \= Lazy results. Good prompts \= Good (not perfect) Results**  
* [**Copy Template \>\>\>**](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1COobluSItCcirJoQmjv3JDFPrqFADGGRXBeqIxnUUJ4/edit?usp=sharing)

 

# **Simple and Effective Copywriting Prompt**

You're an expert copywriter trained to write ecommerce email marketing copy.

Your mission is to write punchy, direct-response friendly email marketing copy tailored for ecommerce brands. The copy must be short, skimmable, and optimized for mobile inboxes, while aligned with brand tone and the customer's journey stage.

Write 3 short and punchy email campaign variations displayed in table format for this consumer product brand.

Each variation must include the following fields in this exact order:

* **Subject Line**  
* **Preview Text**  
* **Headline**  
* **Subheadline**  
* **First CTA (text only, no button styling)**  
* **Body Copy** (2-4 sentences, short, scroll-stopping, mobile-first)  
* **Bridge Section** (Example: “Graphic: Side-by-side results” or “Visual: 5-star review snapshot”)  
* **Product Section** (Highlight 1 product with background info if appropriate such as 3 feature bullets OR 3–6 products with 1-line benefit/feature per product \+ CTA)

**Important Notes:**

* Built for image-based layout (e.g. Figma, Canva)  
* Designed for mobile-first consumption  
* Bold, no fluff, attention-grabbing  
* Optimized for engagement and clicks  
* Must include social proof, direct response copy principles, and objection handling when applicable  
* No discounting unless strategically required  
* Tone and language should reflect the brand as described above

**Here’s what you need to know about the brand and email you are writing about:**

* Brand Website: \[INSERT LINK\]  
* Brand Overview (2-3 sentences): \[Briefly explain who you are, your tone/voice, and what differentiates you\]  
* Customer Insights: \[Describe your customer—what they want, pain points, desired outcomes, and what motivates them to buy\]  
* Email Topic or Campaign Angle: \[e.g. “New product launch,” “FAQ Myth Bust,” “Founder story,” “Us vs. Them,” etc.\]

# Utilizing Infographics

* **To visualize data, simplifying complex ideas, breaking up text, increasing engagement**  
* **Design basics: clean layout, readable, simple**  
* **Great way to explain complex information in a simple, creative visual format. Skimmable, Clear, Engaging**  
* **Types:**   
  * **Checklists**  
  * **Icons**  
  * **Feature Diagrams**  
  * **Timelines**  
  * **Numbered Lists**  
  * **Comparison Charts**  
  * **Tables**  
  * **Flow Charts**  
  * **Graphs**

# Subject Lines & Preview Texts

* Not about your open rates. About the frame of the email & quality of the customer you're attracting  
* **Guidelines**:  
  * Short & punchy: aim for curiosity, urgency, and specificity. Avoid generic “Newsletter” subject lines  
  * Invoke curiosity  
  * Personalization: using names, segments, and past behavior to make the subject feel relevant.  
  * Complementarity with preview text: preview text expands or reinforces the subject line; use wisely (not just filler).  
  * Testing & optimization: A/B test subject lines; track open rates; tweak based on what works.

Remember: people should open your emails based off your sender name, NOT your Subject line. 

Don't overthink it

Link to document in video: [https://gamma.app/docs/Email-Copywriting-3kadzcmu0ixjepr?mode=doc](https://gamma.app/docs/Email-Copywriting-3kadzcmu0ixjepr?mode=doc)

# GAMMA COPY

Email Copywriting

# **Email Copywriting**

# **The Principles of Good Copy**

# **Nobody Is Reading Your Copy**

You might have the best copy in the world.  
But that means nothing when nobody is reading it.  
The average customer today is scared of reading.  
With Tik Tok, Instagram, and Snapchat offering instant dopamine… how can you blame them?  
The average time you have with the customer's attention span has shrunk drastically in the last couple years.  
Before, you could have had an average of 5-10 seconds of attention per email.  
Todat, you *maybe* have 3 seconds.  
**Your ability to get somebody to convert is completely dependent on how fast and effectively you can get your point across.**

# **Email is Unique…**

No one is sitting down to read through their emails like it's social media.  
**They are opening their email in quick bursts.**  
You are in between tasks and open your email for a quick second.  
You get up to go to the bathroom and on your 20 second walk over you check your email.  
You're waiting for your kid's bagel to finish toasting.  
Bottom line, the clock is ticking.  
**You only have a few seconds of your customer's attention.**

# **What To Avoid**

### ❌ **Avoid Unnecessary Information**

A lot of the copy in emails I see is just fluff. Unnecessary.  
For example \- when someone joins a newsletter they KNOW they will be getting exclusive access, insights, sneak peaks, and community. There's no need to waste space telling them about it.  
Instead, focus your time on useful information such as brand unique selling propositions, social proof, etc

### ❌ **Avoid Too Much Copy**

Too much copy —\> increased churn.  
Even if you have helpful info, no one will want to read it. The shorter your copy is the more likely someone will take the time to read it.

### ❌ **Too Many Points**

The customer is easy to get overwhelmed.  
If you try to tell your customer 5 different takeaways, **they won’t remember any of them** \- each takeaway gets 20% of their attention.  
Whereas, if you just try to prove ONE thing and get ONE takeaway, it’s much more likely for the customer to remember it.  
It seems counterproductive to leave out benefits, but we've found it to increase conversions to just focus on single benefits.

**Ex. Unnecessary Information**

**Ex. Too Much Copy**

**Ex. Too Many Points**

# **The Principles of High Converting Copy**

# **Principles of High Converting Copy: S.C.E**

### **S**

**Skimmable**

The ease at which someone can briefly skim your email.

### **C**

**Clear and Concise**

Points are clear and we aren't trying to do too much

### **E**

**Engaging**

The entertainment of content you are sending.

# **Principle \#1: Skimmable**

Time is money\!  
The customer isn’t sitting down every morning and reading their emails in-depth like they would read the morning newspaper.  
We only have a couple seconds of our customer's attention. **If people can't skim your email, they aren't going to bother reading it.** Optimize for the skim, not the read.

### ✅ **Avoid Blocks of Text**

Blocks of text scare people off. It’s the modern customer's kryptonite.  
It’s also difficult for the eyes to make out and process. This will result in people just skipping past your section.  
Try to **shorten your copy or split up your text with line breaks.**

### ✅ **Bold Main Points**

Most of your audience won’t read any of your copy. But they will read your bolded main points as they are skimming your email.  
Use body copy to support your main points for anybody wanting to read further.  
Main points as headlines are used to allow readers to get the full picture, then body copy is used for further justification and proof (just like I've done with this guide).

### ✅ **Keep It Short\!**

We'll touch on this in all our principles… But the shorter your copy is, the more skimmable it is.

**Ex. Splitting Copy Up w/ Bold Main Points**

**Ex. Splitting Copy Up w/ Bold Main Points**

# **Principle \#2: Clear & Concise**

The customer's IQ drops by about 50% the second they open their email inbox.  
They're in zombie brain mode. If we aren't clear with what we're giving them, they won't understand. If we aren't concise, they won't bother giving you the time.

### ✅ **Short and Sweet**

The shorter your email the better.  
We only have a few seconds of our customer's attention so we have to make good use of it.  
Your email ideally **shouldn't be longer than 1-2 scrolls.**

### ✅ **Limit to 1-3 Key Points Per Email**

When you can, try to make your email about one specific takeaway.  
Our odds of having the customer remember **one takeaway** is much higher than the odds of them taking away 3+ takeaways.  
The more concise, the better\!

**Ex. Short and Focused on 1 Key Benefit**

**Ex. Short and Focused on 1 Key Benefit**

# **Principle \#2: Engaging**

The customer is craving dopamine, we need to give it to them.  
We want our emails to be engaging enough to trigger dopamine in the customer’s brain.  
That will lead to a positive association with our brand and result in future opens and sales.

### ✅ **Punchy**

A punchy email is short, but full of power. The copy evokes emotion and the information is dense.  
Inject lots of information into to tight spaces.

### ✅ **Informational**

People love to learn and they want to be able to make an educated decision in their purchasing decision.  
If you can give facts, people will engage with your emails more often.  
If someone learns something new every time they open your email, you're in a good place.

### ✅ **Entertaining**

The bar has been raised in terms of triggering dopamine for users (thanks a lot tik tok).  
Use jokes, puns, puzzles, etc to spice up your copy.  
The worst thing you can do is be repetitive, so get experimental\!

**Ex. Fun and Educational**

**Ex. Creative, Funny, and Punchy**

# **Utilizing Infographics**

# **Why Use Infographics?**

Using infographics check ALL of the core email copy principle boxes:

* Skimmable  ✅  
* Clear & Concise ✅  
* Engaging ✅

They are a natural way to split copy up to avoid blocks of copy.

# **Checklists**

Checklists are a more creative and engaging way to show your benefits. Much better than boring blocks of copy.

# **Icon Graphics**

People loveeeee icons. They're an easy way for the brain to skim through and understand the benefits of your product.

# **Feature Diagrams**

People need visualization to clearly see what they're getting with your product, make it easy for them\!

**Example \#1**

**Example \#2**

# **Timelines**

Map out the customer journey of your product so they're clear on when they will see results.  
You can also use a timeline to showcase the history of your brand or product.

**Example \#1**

**Example \#2**

# **Numbered Lists**

A simple and easy way to display information in a more engaging way than blocks of copy.

**Example \#1**

**Example \#2**

# **Comparison Chart**

The easiest way for people to differentiate is through comparison, lay it out clearly for them.

**Example \#1**

**Example \#2**

# **Tables**

Spreading out info into different sections is easier for the brain to follow and comprehend.

**Example \#1**

**Example \#2**

# **Flow Charts**

These are creative ways to demonstrate the use cases of your different products.

**Example \#1**

**Example \#2**

# **Graphs**

Rather than listing out simple statistics, help the customer visualize them with a graph.

**Example \#1**

**Example \#2**

# **ChatGPT Copywriting**

# **AI Isn't Perfect**

It's easy to tell when something is written by AI… we can all agree there.  
ChatGPT isn't going to give you the finished product for your copy.  
Rather, you should use it as a guide and as a framework.  
Using ChatGPT should be an iterative process where you're making edits, asking it to change things around, etc  
This framework is a good place to start.

# **Simple and Effective Copywriting Prompt**

You're an expert copywriter trained to write ecommerce email marketing copy.  
Your mission is to write punchy, direct-response friendly email marketing copy tailored for ecommerce brands. The copy must be short, skimmable, and optimized for mobile inboxes, while aligned with brand tone and the customer's journey stage.  
Write 3 short and punchy email campaign variations displayed in table format for this consumer product brand.  
Each variation must include the following fields in this exact order:

* **Subject Line**  
* **Preview Text**  
* **Headline**  
* **Subheadline**  
* **First CTA (text only, no button styling)**  
* **Body Copy** (2-4 sentences, short, scroll-stopping, mobile-first)  
* **Bridge Section** (Example: “Graphic: Side-by-side results” or “Visual: 5-star review snapshot”)  
* **Product Section** (Highlight 1 product with background info if appropriate such as 3 feature bullets OR 3–6 products with 1-line benefit/feature per product \+ CTA)

**Important Notes:**

* Built for image-based layout (e.g. Figma, Canva)  
* Designed for mobile-first consumption  
* Bold, no fluff, attention-grabbing  
* Optimized for engagement and clicks  
* Must include social proof, direct response copy principles, and objection handling when applicable  
* No discounting unless strategically required  
* Tone and language should reflect the brand as described above

**Here’s what you need to know about the brand and email you are writing about:**

* Brand Website: \[INSERT LINK\]  
* Brand Overview (2-3 sentences): \[Briefly explain who you are, your tone/voice, and what differentiates you\]  
* Customer Insights: \[Describe your customer—what they want, pain points, desired outcomes, and what motivates them to buy\]  
* Email Topic or Campaign Angle: \[e.g. “New product launch,” “FAQ Myth Bust,” “Founder story,” “Us vs. Them,” etc.\]

## **Use My AI To Help Write Copy**

Lucky for you, I've created the **Email Marketing Brain…**  
Which is a custom GPT trained with over 500 pages of email marketing knowledge which can be used to write copy, make calendars, come up with email ideas, and answer email questions you may have.  
It's free\! You can [**​claim access here »​**](https://wellcopy.net/gpt)  
Here's how the process and output look…

## **Writing / Mapping Out Your Copy**

We notice a lot of disconnect between copy and the design when we just write copy in table format.  
Instead, our copywriters act as "Email Architects" and map out exactly how the email should look like.  
This gives copy more control over the finished design product as well as giving the design direction.  
Our final products became noticeably better when we started formatting things this way.

**Example \#1**

**Example \#2**

# **Subject Lines and Preview Texts**

# **IMPORTANT: Your Subject Line and Preview Text Shouldn't Matter for Getting Opens**

Your content should be so good…  
That customers are conditioned to open every email no matter what.  
The goal is to **get people to open emails based off your sender name.**  
I've never had a subject line genuinely get an impactful difference on an open rate… at most you can get \~ 10% jump in opens.  
**So what’s the point of SLs and PTs if they aren’t for opens?**  
More sales.  
Different SLs and PTs will change the frame of your email and prime the customer for sales in different ways.  
We’ve had some SLs and PTs generate 3x more than others (same email copy and design) in A/B tests.  
Test your SL styles based off revenue, and less about opens.  
**Example Below: Subject line A/B test resulting in 3x more sales.**

# **Simple Subject Line Framework**

Here’s a framework to follow that looks clean, builds intrigue, and will prime your customer for a purchase.

* 2-5 words in Length  
* Title Case, Like This Sentence  
* Builds curiosity  
* Ends with 1-2 emojis (optional)

Examples:

* A Taste of Sunshine ☀️  
* Myth. Busted. 👀  
* They’re Finally Here 🤩  
* It's Literally This Easy 🤣

# **Simple Preview Text Framework**

Don’t overthink it.  
Preview texts are just building off of the SL and creating more curiosity.  
Here’s a framework to follow that feeds off of the SL:

* One full sentence long  
* Regular capitalization  
* Adds support to the SL  
* Ends with … (optional)

Examples:

* This wasn’t supposed to be made, but now it’s a bestseller…  
* It’s available, but the last drop sold out in 24 hours…  
* If you want the cold hard facts, we got them…  
* No one is talking about this but it will make you more money...

# **Don't Overthink It**

If you send good content, people will open your emails so matter what.

# Uploading Designs From Figma To Klaviyo

1. Slice your emails into different sections using Figma's slice tool  
2. Save your sections as images  
3. Compress your images using a site like [https://www.iloveimg.com/compress-image](https://www.iloveimg.com/compress-image)  
4. Upload your sections as images into Klaviyo  
5. Add your links and alt texts :)

# GAMMA DESIGN

Email Design

# **Email Design**

# **The Importance Of Design**

# **Design Matters**

In 2025+, new ecom brands are popping up every day.  
These brands are all setting up email, since they know it's the backbone of every brand.  
Many will stick to basic templates and lazy designs resulting in brands looking exactly like the thousands of others in their niche.  
Design is how you differentiate.  
It's how you stand out from your competition and make sales in a competitive market.  
Branded, conversion based designs (outlined in this doc) have resulted in **3x in revenue** for emails we've tested.

# **More Revenue**

Great designs will maximize revenue through conversion based principles.  
We've seen design A/B tests result in 3x more revenue just by optimizing spacing, sizing, and style.  
Great looking email designs are exponential in returns.  
Mediocre designs will lead to customers opening and bouncing out of the email.

# **List Engagement**

We want to get customers "addicted" to opening our emails.  
Obviously no one is at the edge of their seat waiting every minute for you to send an email.  
But by associating dopamine releases with pretty designs, we get continued engagement.  
Sending the same stuff over and over again will miss this opportunity and lead to churn.

# **Deliverability**

With more engagement, comes improved deliverability.  
With each open and each click we earn trust points with Google, Yahoo, etc  
We continue to prove to them that we are a reputable sender and increase our inbox placement rate.

# **Differentiation**

Templatized designs and basic layouts make you look like every other brand out there.  
New brands are popping of every day.  
Differentiating yourself with your content is the only way to stay ahead of these new stores.  
If not, you'll get lost in the mix and no one will buy from you or remember you.

# **Authority**

We judge books by their covers, it's human instinct.  
Would you trust the word of someone in a suit or someone in baggy / dirty clothes?  
Probably the person in the suit.  
Same applies for your brand.  
People are more likely to buy from sophisticated and well kept brands.  
Having basic, mediocre designs will decrease the value of your brand and your word.  
Customers subconsciously pay attention to these things.

# **Branding**

Pretty website, pretty ads, then ugly email designs.  
Customers make positive associations in your ads and websites that will be lost if your emails aren't matching.  
In fact, it will harm your conversions.  
Branding must be congruent across all channels to maximize revenue and keep your brand image strong.

# **The Principles of Good Design**

## **Email Design That Drives Clicks**

Pretty doesn’t equal profit.  
Your email has one job: **Get the click.**  
Here’s how to design your emails to hand the click to the customer on a silver platter:

* **Button Above the Fold** – Most users won’t scroll. Put a CTA button in the hero section so every reader sees it immediately.  
* **Large, Thumb-Friendly Buttons** – Think mobile-first. Buttons should be at least 1.5–2 inches wide so they’re easy to tap.  
* **Clear, Repeating CTAs** – Don’t just place one button at the end. Repeat your CTA 2–3 times throughout the email.  
* **Center Your Buttons** – Most people hold their phone in their right hand. Centered buttons reduce friction and boost taps.  
* **Buttons for Each Product** – If you’re showing multiple products, each one should have its own “Shop Now” or “View Product” button underneath.  
* **End with a General CTA** – Not every product will land. Always close with a “Shop All” or “Browse More” button for catch-all clicks.  
* **Make CTAs Stand Out** – Use high-contrast colors and simple backgrounds. Your buttons should be the most obvious thing in the email.

#### **Great Examples That Follow Best Practices**

# **Principle \#1: Ease Of Click**

When customers are opening emails they are in zombie mode.  
Any friction in the ability for someone to click will automatically lead to churn.  
We need to hand the click to them on a silver platter with the following:

#### ✅ **Button Above the Fold**

The "above the fold" section is the section of the email that is viewed without scrolling.  
Not every customer will scroll on your email, so you want a button available in the hero section.  
This way EVERY customer has the ability to visit the site and go down the funnel.  
Including a top button is an easy low-hanging fruit to automatically improve your click rates.

#### ✅ **Large Buttons**

An iPhone screen is 2.75 inches. That's not a large space.  
Larger buttons, more surface area, easier ability to click. This has a massive impact on conversions.

#### ✅ **Clear Calls to Action**

The goal of an email is to get someone to click.  
Your buttons should be the easiest to view part of your email and there should be no ability for someone to click over a button. Avoid distracting backgrounds and use contrasting colors to get your CTAs to stand out.

#### ✅ **Centered Main Buttons**

Most people hold their phone in their right hand.  
If you put a button on the left side, the user has to stretch their thumb over to click (more friction). This actually negatively impacts conversions substantially.

✅ **Button Above The Fold**

✅ **Large Centered Button**

❌ **Unclear, Small Button**

**Uncentered, Small Button**

# **Principle \#2: Skimmability**

Nobody is sitting at their desk annotating their emails.  
They're checking emails as they walk to the bathroom, on a quick break, etc  
On average you have 2-4 seconds to get your point across.  
Optimize for the skim, not for the read.

#### ✅ **Simplicity**

Simplicity always wins. Adding too much complexity in your emails will overwhelm the customer.  
More importantly, complex designs will distract from the whole purpose of the email… to sell. Keep it simple so users can easily skim and understand the contents of the email.

#### ✅ **Smooth, Clear Sections**

The human brain likes when things are organized and clear.  
We want to be smooth with our sections, but clear when we are differentiating. This way, people can clearly see 2-3 sections, categorize in their head, and skim with ease

#### ✅ **Highlighted Main Points**

Very few people will actually ready your body copy in full.  
But they WILL read / skim your headlines / subheadlines If we only get 3 seconds of our viewers attention we need to make it loud and clear what our main points are.

#### ✅ **Infographics**

We talked about this before in the copy section… but we need to use infographics to mix our copy with visual elements that people can skim.

✅ **Simple \+ Use Of Infographic**

✅ **Simple \+ Highlighted Main Points**

❌ **Too Complex**

# **Principle \#3: Branding**

A design can be beautiful, but unrelated to a brand.  
The strengthen your brand you need congruency across all channels.  
Without it, positive associations with the brand from other channels will be lost in email.  
This will promote long term conversions.

* Branded graphics  
* Correct fonts  
* Congruent styling

# **Mastering The Different Email Sections**

# **The Different Email Sections**

We have the hero, the bridge, product section, and footer.  
Not every email will have a bridge or a product section, but most will.  
Here's an example:

# **Hero Section Mastery**

The hero section is the top part of the email. It is by far **the most important.**  
Most people will only read the top section so 75% of your efforts should go to this.

#### ✅ **Optimize Above the Fold**

The "Above The Fold" section is the section of the email that is viewed without scrolling.  
To remove friction for the buyer, we want the above the fold to have everything the customer needs.  
A great "Above The Fold" includes:

* Clear headline  
* Strong graphic  
* Clear value prop  
* Clear button

#### ✅ **Top Button**

As mentioned before, not every customer will scroll on your email, so you want a button available in the hero section.  
This way EVERY customer has the ability to visit the site and go down the funnel. Including a top button is an easy low-hanging fruit to automatically improve your click rates.

✅ **Optimized Hero Section**

✅ **Optimized Hero Section**

# **Bridge Section**

Your bridge section is the supporting section to your Hero.  
It will also serve as a transition into a specific product section if you include one. In some cases you won't have a bridge, in some you'll have two.  
The bridge can be anything…

* Supporting copy  
* Supporting graphic  
* Testimonial(s)  
* Photo(s)  
* Feature(s)  
* Steps / Instructions  
* Tips

**In most cases, we want to use the Infographics as shown in the "Copy" modules.**  
Here are some other simple examples:

# **Product Section**

Your product section is exactly as it sounds.  
It's the part of the email that is going to specifically feature and highlight a product or products. Products can even be categories, not necessarily specific products.

#### ✅ **Individual Calls To Action**

It's important if your showing products to make it clear that the customer can click it and go to that product page.  
This removes friction for the buyer so they don't have to go to the general site and find the product. Make it clear by giving individual buttons or underlines product titles.

#### ✅ **End With A General CTA**

After you give individual products, it's important to always have a general CTA below.  
Maybe the customer is interested, but didn't like any of the products in the email. By leaving out a general CTA below, you lose out on all these customers. We've noticed 25% boosts in clicks by doing this.

#### **Optimized Product Section Example:**

# **Footer**

Your footer is going to be universal across all your emails.  
Most brands mess this up… It's simple simple to master and is a huge lever for getting extra clicks.  
We want to include buttons in our footer to allow for further options for the customer.  
What if the customer scrolls your whole email and doesn't find a product they like? Well if they scroll to the footer they may see a product category they want to browse\!

**Good Example \#1**

**Good Example \#2**

# **Email Transitions**

Transitions are what help make your different sections in your email flow.  
Having separate sections that are blocky and unrelated will lead to churn.  
By making it congruent, customers will keep scrolling without realizing.

# DELIVERABILITY

# What is Deliverability?

**Link to document in video: [https://gamma.app/docs/Deliverability-gflvd38h9vy6v7c](https://gamma.app/docs/Deliverability-gflvd38h9vy6v7c)** 

* Deliverability determines where your emails are landing: Primary tab, Promotions tab, or Spam  
* Sender reputation: Your domain's "credit score", determined by google analyzing the engagement/interest on your emails (opens, clicks, responses, etc.)  
* Technical Setup: [Setting up branded sending domain \>\>\>](https://help.klaviyo.com/hc/en-us/articles/115000357752)  
* Building your reputation  
  * Start small & build up (volume)  
  * Get good engagement, build the audience & loyalty  
  * Ramp up to find your engaged users  
* 90 Day engaged list will be your bread and butter  
* Exclude profiles that are not engaged, ESPECIALLY important in the beginning

# GAMMA DELIVERABILITY

Deliverability

# **Deliverability**

# **Intro Deliverability**

# **What is Deliverability?**

Inbox providers want to improve the experience of their customers by only showing them emails they want to see.  
Deliverability essentially refers to your domain and IP's sending "credit score".  
If you have a good credit score you'll land in the **primary tab**.  
If you have a bad credit score you'll land in **spam**.  
If you're somewhere in the middle you'll land in the **promotions tab**.

# **Just Think in the Eyes of Google…**

If 2/10 people are opening your emails… you think they'll keep putting you in their main inbox?

# **What Affects Deliverability?**

### **Technical Setup**

Fancy records hosted by your domain provider in your DNS settings (MX, SPF, DMARC, DKIM)

\+ Dedicated Klaviyo sending domain

### **Sender Reputation**

A combination of your email engagement metrics (open rates, click rates, bounce rates, spam complaints)

\+ Proper domain warm-up

# **Technical Setup**

Seems complex, but most domains already have the correct records in place on your domain, maybe one missing.  
**As you setup Klaviyo they set these all up for you :)**  
Records you need on your domain for inbox placement:

* SPF  
* DMARC  
* DKIM

Use [**​h​**](https://glockapps.com/)**[​https://glockapps.com/domain-checker​](https://glockapps.com/domain-checker)** to see if you are missing any, if you are follow their guide to getting installed.  
Feel free to message our team as well if you have issues with this.

# **How To Get Good Deliverability**

# **Building A Reputation**

The most important thing you need to have to land in the inbox is a **reputation**.  
When you just start sending emails, you have no reputation. **You need to build it.**  
How do you build a reputation?

* Start by sending emails at low volume  
* Get good engagement on your emails you're sending  
* Slowly ramp up volume to engaged users to continue building your credibility

# **All That Matters For Deliverability**

Engagement rates.  
That is all that Google, Yahoo, etc look at.  
Open rates, click rates, bounce rates, unsubscribe rates, and spam complaint rates.  
Send to the right people with good content → Receive positive engagement → Increase deliverability

| Metric Name | Metric Value Goal |
| ----- | ----- |
| Open Rates | Greater than 50% |
| Click Rates | Greater than 0.75% |
| Bounce Rate | Less than 1% |
| Spam Complaint Rate | Less than 0.01% |
| Unsubscribe Rate (doesn't affect deliverability) | Less than 0.4% |

# **Only Send To Engaged People**

If we send emails to our whole list we will be sending to people who will never open an email from us… they changed emails, they stopped checking, lost interest in your brand, or maybe even they gave you a fake email.  
Just simply send emails to your engaged people, people who have recently engaged with emails.  
This is how we can get consistent 50% open rates.  
Use either a 30, 60, 90, 120, 180, or 365 day engaged list.  
Whatever list gets you 50-60% opens.  
If you start to get 60%+ opens, widen your list to a larger timeframe to get more opportunities for revenue.  
If you start to get 40% opens, tighten your list to a smaller timeframe.  
This is all you need to maintain deliverability.

| Segment Name | Segment Definition | Segment Use Case |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| **90 Day Engaged List** (You can use any time frame, 90 is recommended to start) | Someone can receive email marketing because person is subscribed**AND** Someone has Opened Email at least once in the last 90 days**OR** Someone has been Active on Site at least once in the last 90 days**OR** Someone has Placed An Order at least once in the last 90 days | This is your base segment for sending all your email campaigns to. We want to only send emails to our active subscribers so we keep high open rates and click rates. If we send to unengaged people they will hurt our engagement rates, Google will notice, and we will land more often in the spam folder. |

# **Make Better Emails\!**

You make good emails… people enjoy them… and continue to open them\!

# **Uploading Emails for Proper Deliverability**

# **How To Properly Upload Emails To Klaviyo for Deliverability**

* Slice email design into sections and download the sections as JPG or PNG (doesn't really matter)  
* Upload the slices into [**​https://www.iloveimg.com/compress-image​**](https://www.iloveimg.com/compress-image) to decrease file size  
* Upload compressed slices into your email platform  
* Add alt text to all of your slices  
* Add a link to all of your slices

# **Check Your Deliverability**

# **Check Your Deliverability With Glockapps**

Glockapps is a platform that allows you to see where you are landing in inboxes.  
Main inbox, promotions, or spam.  
It will give you a list of 100+ email addresses, you will send a campaign to this list, then they will send back to you how often you landed in the different inboxes.  
If you struggle with deliverability, consider using one of their tests once per month.  
Deliverability

* 90% of results are driven by basics, 10% come from the advanced 'stuff.'  
* That 'stuff' is A/B testing & optimizations  
* Possibilities are endless  
* Review the attached resources to find what tests are optimal for your brand\!

# GAMMA OPTIMIZATION

A/B Testing & Optimization

# **A/B Testing & Optimization**

# **Intro**

# **You Can't Conversion Rate Optimize Your Way To a 10x…**

90% of the results are driven by the basics.  
10% come from the advanced stuff.  
It's important to not get too carried away with A/B testing if you don't have all the cores in place.  
Focus on high levers first and spend your time there.

# **How Do We Optimize?**

A/B tests\!  
It doesn't matter what you think.  
Numbers don't lie.  
Test as much as you can when you have time and apply your learnings.  
We'll be going over the highest leverage A/B tests and how to set them up.

# **Flows / Campaigns Optimization**

# **The Possibilities Are Endless**

You can literally test anything.  
However, it's important to make sure you have the proper volume to know your results are conclusive.  
If you need to send multiple emails to get enough data to make a conclusion, that's fine.  
Since you can test anything, it's hard to choose.  
First, you want to test the highest leverage tests, which I will be sharing with you.

# **Campaign Send Time**

Different lists will respond better at different times of the day.  
It's generally good practice to send at the same time for most your campaigns so customers get accustomed to receiving your emails at a certain part of their day.  
Some lists respond well in the morning, others in the afternoon, and others at night.  
Typically, we've found around 11am-12pm to perform the best.  
Main times to test would be 9am, 12pm, 2pm, and 4pm.

# **Graphic vs Text Based**

Heavy text based emails vs graphic based emails can really vary across accounts.  
Test out different styles to see what your list responds to better.  
Especially for big drops and sales…  
We find that you want to mix them up but use text based for key emails.  
Example of Text Based sale email winner:

# **Promoting Categories vs Products**

We've noticed that some brand customers prefer to shop in their emails by category and some by individual products.  
Important to mix it up but you can bias one option more, especially for important emails,  if it performs better.  
Example of categories performing better:

# **SLs and PTs**

This is the obvious one, but hard to take the learnings and apply to future emails since there will be a lot of variation.  
Here are some tests you can run:

* Including discount vs not including (for sales)  
* Including or excluding emojis  
* Using "…" or exlcuding  
* Adding time delays  
* Using all caps vs not

# **Flow Time Delays**

This is the biggest lever I'd say.  
Testing the amount of time from the action of the customer and the first email they receive.  
Then also, the time delay between your flow messages.

# **Other High-Impact A/B Tests to Run**

* **Show Product Prices vs. Hide Them**  
* Why: Showing the price sets expectations early. Hiding it may drive curiosity and clicks. Measure both CTR and conversion rate.  
* **Graphic vs. Plain Text vs. Branded Plain Text**  
* Why: The email format impacts how trustworthy and engaging it feels. Test to find what your audience responds to best.  
* **Clear Discount vs. Mystery Discount**  
* Why: Clarity removes friction, but mystery can tap into curiosity and boost urgency. See which one drives more conversions.  
* **Lifestyle Imagery vs. Product Imagery**  
* Why: Lifestyle photos may create a stronger desire to purchase, but sometimes simple visuals outperform. Test both.  
* **Long-Form vs. Short-Form Emails**  
* Why: Different products and audiences respond to different formats. Find the ideal balance of info and brevity.  
* **Send Time and Day of Week**  
* Why: Your audience may engage better at specific times. Use data to uncover hidden timing windows that improve open and click rates.  
* **CTA Link Placement**  
* Why: Different subscribers scroll and consume content differently. CTA placement can significantly influence clicks.  
* **Subject Line Framing: Curiosity vs. Clarity**  
* Why: Curiosity may boost opens. Clarity may increase conversions. Test which one aligns better with your funnel goals.  
* **From Name and Sender Identity**  
* Why: Sender identity impacts trust and open rates. The right sender name might boost both engagement and deliverability.  
* **CTA Text**  
* Why: Language subtly shapes expectations. Some CTAs push urgency, others are more inviting. Small changes can mean big results.  
* **Visual Format: GIF vs. Static Image**  
* Why: GIFs can attract attention but may also slow load times. Static images may be more direct and conversion-focused.  
* **Personalization Depth**  
* Why: Personalized emails often feel more relevant, but sometimes they’re ignored or seem gimmicky. Test if it truly improves engagement.  
* **Email Length: Short vs. Long-Form**  
* Why: Short emails may drive faster clicks. Longer emails may convert better after building more context. Test both to see what works best for your audience.

A/B Testing & Optimization

# **Video Training**

# **Why This Is Important**

# **SMS Marketing Is The Future**

I mean, who doesn’t use text messages… and check them every day…  
**SMS marketing messages have an average open rate of 98%** (email has an average open rate of 30%)  
When you click send, you can almost guarantee that your customers… people who ALREADY know like and trust you, will see your message.  
Similar to email, it is the quickest and easiest access to people who have proven they are ready to buy.  
Building a strong sms list is an ASSET that no one can take away.  
Zuck can’t take away your sms list or shut down your account like with paid ads.

# **Setting Up SMS**

# **How To Get Set Up**

Now, you can’t send sms messages without an SMS provider.  
These softwares allow you to setup a phone number to send messages, setup automations, and track your data.  
**In my opinion there’s no right answer, all the main platforms pretty much do the same things.**  
Just pick one and go from there, you really can’t go wrong.

# **Klaviyo**

If you use Klaviyo for email marketing I would recommend using them for SMS as well to consolidate your platforms.  
It may be a little bit more expensive but in my opinion it is worth it to have everything under one roof.  
It has all the base features you need and is simple to follow.

# **Other SMS Platform Options (It Really Doesn't Matter Which You Use)**

Attentive and Postscript are the two other main options and both are great.  
They’ll all try to sell you on advanced AI features and stuff they have that the others don’t… but in reality you never really use those features and they’re all pretty much the same and relatively same price.  
Each platform will have pretty straightforward tutorials and guides on how to get your account and number setup, so I won’t cover that.

# **How To Grow Your SMS List**

# **Checkout Page SMS Sign Up**

This is a simple checkbox at checkout which will sign up someone's number for receiving marketing.  
SUPER low hanging fruit that takes 5 minutes to set up on Shopify.  
We want it to be auto-checked so someone will be added to the list.  
This allows you to always grab info and market to the people who already know, like and trust you.  
These will be your highest value members.

# **Instructions for Post Purchase Opt-Ins**

* From your Shopify admin, go to **Settings** \> [**​Checkout​**](https://admin.shopify.com/settings/checkout).  
* To add a sign-up checkbox to your checkout, in the **Marketing options** section, check **Email**.  
* Check **Preselected** so that the email marketing sign-up check box is preselected at the checkout by default for customers without an account or customers who are on your email subscription list. The email marketing sign-up check box isn't preselected for customers who have opted out of email marketing or who aren't on your email subscription list.  
* Click **Save**.

# **Pop-Up Forms**

Pop-up forms are the forms that show up on your site asking for your email address in exchange for some sort of incentive.  
Some may say they are "intrusive" but the ROI through getting customer info and send marketing to increase LTV is too big to ignore (+ a good form won't be intrusive).

# **Ask For Email THEN SMS**

For our pop-ups **we don't want to add both email and sms in the same step.**  
Why?  
It's perceived to be **much more work** for the customer and it will hurt conversions.  
We want to ask for email first so the customer **thinks all they have to do it enter their email,** then after we ask for their number and say something like "finish claiming your discount by entering your phone number"  
This will get much higher conversions.

**Step 1**

**Step 2**

**Mobile:**  
**Desktop:**

# **Pop-Up Form Mastery 1 Hour Video**

# **SMS Content / Nuances**

# **Frequency**

Sms marketing is a bit more intrusive than something like email marketing.  
With email  you can easily send 4-5 email campaigns per week without getting much backlash from customers.  
For this reason, **unsubscribe rates are much higher for SMS marketing.**  
We want to send much less frequently and only send important messages to avoid annoying your list and keeping unsubscribe rates low.  
**I wouldn’t recommend sending more than 1-2 sms messages per week** (we notice results plateau and unsubscribe rates increase past this)  
If you are sending more you need to make sure you’re really segmenting your list to only send to extremely engaged segments.

## **Join My Email Course / Community**

* 30+ Module Email course  
* 250+ Winning Flow and Campaign Email Templates  
* Weekly Live Calls  
* Community of Ecom Brands & Marketers For 24/7 Support

[**​Join Community »​**](https://www.skool.com/email-marketerz/about)

# **MMS vs SMS Messages**

SMS messages are the regular messages that are just plain text.  
MMS messages are the ones that include an image or a gif in the message.  
**MMS messages are usually 2-3x MORE expensive than sending an sms.**  
Meaning, if you want to get a better ROI on spend you’d have to make 2x-3x more revenue with your mms messages to outperform sms message ROI.  
In my experience that’s very rare.  
I would highly recommend just **sticking to SMS text only messages** to get the highest ROI from your marketing.

# **Message Length**

In each SMS you only have 160 characters, which is NOT a lot…  
You can go over, but it's more expensive.  
If you go over by even 1 character to 161, **the price of your message doubles.**  
You do not have a lot to work with here… your messages need to be very short and concise.  
In my experience it’s very unlikely the increased text will result in 2x more revenue and ROI for you.  
**So, I would not recommend going over 160 characters, just keep your messages super short and waste no space to save on costs.**

**1 SMS Long**

**3 SMS Long (triple the cost)**

# **Emojis** 🤪

It would be amazing to be able to spice up your messages with eye-catching emojis…  
However. emojis eat up your character limit.  
They use a different kind of code and can have the **equivalent of 35-50 characters in your message**, which you already don’t have a lot of space in.  
I would recommend NOT using emojis because of this…  
They will just drive your price up and decrease your sms ROI.

# **SMS is Tough\!**

With all your character limits and limitations on images and emojis, your sms messaging has to be pretty bare bones and simple.  
That doesn't mean it can’t be effective… (like the below contributing to 15% of total store revenue)

# **SMS Flows**

# **What Are Flows?**

SMS flows are the automated messages that will fire when a customer completes a specified action.  
Flows like the welcome flow and cart abandon flow  will pick up a ton of revenue for you.  
All of the main platforms make this really easy for you to setup your flows.

# **Keep Them Short and Sweet**

Rather than create long flows of 8-10 messages like we usually do for email, our sms flows have to be minimal.  
We don't want to barge the customer with a ton of texts and risk getting unsubscribes.

# **Welcome Flow**

This flow is going to fire after someone signs up to your SMS list.  
In most cases this will be people who join via the pop-up form you have setup.  
This person will also be receiving the email welcome flow, so don't try to do anything fancy with education or anything like that.  
I like to simply do welcome message with the discount then a closer saying the discount is expiring.

## **Copy Template**

**Welcome SMS 1**

Welcome to \_\_\_\! \[Quick branded remark\]. Use code \_\_\_ for \_\_ OFF your first order\!  
Shop Now: \[link\]  
**Wait 5 days**  
**Welcome SMS 2**  
Hey there, we noticed you signed up, but haven't shopped \_\_ OFF yet. Don't miss out, this offer is about to expire\!  
Shop Now: \[link\]

# **Browse Abandon Flow**

This flow fires for anybody who views an item but doesn't add an item to cart or make a purchase.  
This is one that not everybody has but is extremely profitable for getting customers re-engaged.  
For this message, you can A/B test including a picture of the item the person browsed to help them remember what they looked at. Keep in mind this is 2x-3x more expensive than just using text so review results accordingly.

## **Copy Template**

**Wait 60 minutes from Browse Event**  
**Browse Abandon SMS**  
Have your eye on something? You have good taste ;)  
Keep Shopping: \[Link\]

# **Cart / Checkout Abandon Flows**

You want to setup two different flows for cart and checkout abandon (different events).  
In an ideal world I would love to send multiple cart abandon sms messages, but it's actually illegal to do in the US lol… so we're only limited to one message.  
Don't waste any time in this message, I found that just addressing the abandonment gets the best results.

## **Copy Template**

**Wait 30 minutes from Event**  
**Cart / Checkout Abandon SMS**  
It looks like you forgot something in your cart\!  
Finish Your Order: \[link\]

# **Winback Flow**

This is optional to include… the customer will be receiving your important messages via sms campaigns and many will convert from that.  
This in place will help personalize the customer experience a bit and get some conversions via discounts if you use them.  
You could alternatively exclude the discount and keep it general.

## **Copy Template**

**Wait 120 Days from Last Purchase**  
In the market for a NEW \[product\]?  
We miss you, take 10% OFF with code: \[code\]  
Shop 10% OFF:

# **SMS Campaign Strategy**

# **Keep It Light\!**

We already talked a lot about our SMS content and how often we want to send.  
We should be very careful with what sms messages we’re sending and how often we’re sending.  
With email, we see the best results and engagement sending 4x per week (every other day).  
But for sms we see the best results sending only 1-2 times per week.

# **What To Send**

The messages we do send should be important… such as drops, announcements, special offers, and product highlights.  
With sms we don’t send nurture, social proof, or engagement based content.  
Purely transactional content.  
Sales, flash discounts, new product drops, restocks, etc

# **Example Calendar**

Let's say we have a brand with a new collection launch on October 10th and a flash sale for Halloween…  
Here's what a solid monthly SMS calendar may look like (below)  
Notice the use of reminders and filling in an empty space with a product restock.  
Restocks are a great way to get traction when you aren't sure what to send.

# **Send Times**

Typically I recommend midday from 11am-2pm or evening around 5pm is when we see the best engagement.  
But, every audience is different and you should **test it.**  
Here are some general tips…

* Avoid sending before 10am (some people sleep in)  
* Avoid messages past 7:30pm  
* "Last Chance" sms messages can perform really well at a later time such as 6:30pm-7pm  
* Try to keep most your sends in the base window of 11am \- 2pm as that is lunch time and when many people are checking their phones

# **SMS Swipefile**

# **30 SMS Campaigns Swipe File**

I went through Attentive's SMS database and picked 30 of my favorite SMS messages that are simple but effective.  
Don't be afraid to take inspiration from brands that are bigger than yours (they might know what's working\!)
